## 1. The precise scope and conclusions

Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be a finite nonnegative physical word. For a
convex differentiable function f with 1-Lipschitz gradient and attained
minimum, the actual trajectory is x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t). Its
closest-distance payoff is

    E(f,h)=2(f(x_n)-min f)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2.          (1)

The symbol W_n(h) denotes the supremum of this payoff over all such
functions, initial points with positive distance, and finite Euclidean
dimensions. None of the upper bounds below is an upper bound on W_n.

This manuscript analyzes the SAME two explicitly defined static spiral
realizations as the source, at the fixed advertised smoothness L=1.
We derive their full minimizer sets for every checkpoint count, their
exact or finite-projection nearest-distance structure, and their actual
terminal payoffs. The comparison theorem is deliberately restricted to
ZERO OR ONE selected checkpoint. It includes every eligible position,
every independent parameter pair kappa>0, 0<epsilon<=1/2, and every
actual ordered block at that position. There is no assertion about the
optimized family with two or more checkpoints.

For lambda=1+sqrt(2), fix 0<tau<=1/4 and an integer m>=1536. Define

    beta=1-tau/m, c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    H_(m,tau)=H_m, n_m=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j.             (2)

Every occurrence of a child H_j uses this SAME enclosing pair (m,tau).
Let H_base(h)=1/(1+2 sum h). The joint comparison conclusion is:

    E(f,h)<(441/500) H_base(h)                         (3)

for EVERY one-checkpoint realization of EITHER kind below on
h=H_(m,tau), using that realization's own eligibility threshold.
The two zero-checkpoint realizations have payoffs H_base(h) and
1/[2(1+sum h)], respectively. Consequently the supremum over these
actual zero-or-one-checkpoint functions, normalized by their TRUE
closest-minimizer distances, is exactly

    L_actual,<=1(H_(m,tau))=H_base(H_(m,tau)).           (4)

In particular the normalization improvement does not repair the
small-tau benchmark deficit in this precisely delimited subfamily:

    B_(n_m) L_actual,<=1(H_(m,1/4))<237/250,
    B_(n_m) L_actual,<=1(H_(m,tau))<79/80
                              if 1/5<=tau<=1/4.       (5)

For fixed 0<tau<=1/4 the exact limit is Z_1/(2 exp(tau)).
B_n and Z_1 are the ORIGINAL accepted benchmark quantities defined in
Section9. These conclusions do not reuse the old reduced-channel
estimate to upper-bound actual payoffs.

Two actual boundary tests delimit the argument. With two source-format
checkpoints, copying the one-checkpoint radius can overstate its square
by a factor of five. Also two ordinary blocks with the same sum can
have different backward P,Q outputs. Neither test is an escape on the
small-tau family or a counterexample to the full construction.

## 2. Arc, backward chronology, and actual displacement

Fix independent 0<epsilon<=1/2 and kappa>0. Write

    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    C=c_sp=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa theta_sp),
    R(theta)=C exp(-kappa theta),
    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    v(theta)=R(theta)e(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)),
    n(theta)=e(theta)+kappa t(theta), 0<=theta<=theta_sp.

C here is an arc constant, distinct from the benchmark C_n.
The endpoints are v(0)=(C,0) and v(theta_sp)=(epsilon,-1).
Let K be the convex hull of 0 and this compact arc. For
delta=phi-theta,

    <n(theta),v(phi)>/R(theta)
         =exp(-kappa delta)(cos delta+kappa sin delta).

Its derivative is -(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa delta)sin delta, so its
maximum on the relevant interval (-pi/2,pi/2) is1 at delta=0.
Its value at the extra point0 is0. Thus n(theta) supports K at
v(theta). The vector (0,1) also supports K at v(0).
Furthermore

    p'=-R(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,
    q'=R(cos theta-kappa sin theta),
    p>=epsilon>0, q>=0, p>=epsilon q.                 (6)

For an actual chronological ordinary block alpha_1,...,alpha_r,
alpha_j>=0, construct backward states as follows. Set
r_r=v_r=(epsilon,-1). Given r_j=(u_j,-y_j) and its projected arc
point v_j=v(theta_j), let alpha=alpha_j. If

    y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)C],                        (7)

put v_(j-1)=v(0), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(0). Otherwise take the unique
theta in (0,theta_j] satisfying

    <r_j,e(theta)-kappa^(-1)t(theta)>/R(theta)=1-alpha, (8)

and put v_(j-1)=v(theta), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(theta).
These are exact implicit definitions, never executed root searches.

Here is a full projection and existence proof. Inductively

    u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon,           (9)

and, before a cap,

    r_j=R(theta_j)[(1+t_j)e(theta_j)+kappa t_j t(theta_j)],
    t_j>=0.                                           (10)

They hold at the endpoint with t_r=0. In (7), set
mu=u_j+(alpha-1)C. Then mu>=y_j/kappa>0 and
r_(j-1)-v(0)=mu n(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j), a nonnegative combination
of supporting normals. This proves projection at the cap. The new
inequality y_(j-1)<=kappa(u_(j-1)-C) makes every earlier step capped.

If (7) fails, the point is uncapped. The left side of (8) equals1
at theta_j, is less than1-alpha at0, and has strictly positive
derivative (kappa+kappa^(-1))(u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)/R(theta).
Thus the stated root exists and is unique, including alpha=0 at the
current endpoint. The polar angle of r_j is at least theta_j by(10),
so <r_j,t(theta)> >=0 at this earlier angle. Equation(8) makes
r_j+(alpha-1)v(theta) a nonnegative multiple of n(theta), proving
projection and(10) at the preceding state. Adding alpha(p,-q), using
(6), preserves(9). No chronological step or zero is removed.

For the actual outputs define

    P=sum_j alpha_j p(theta_(j-1)),
    Q=sum_j alpha_j q(theta_(j-1)), s=sum_j alpha_j.

Then

    r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), P>=epsilon Q, 0<=P<=Cs.      (11)

We also need the uniform Q bound used to define the source's scales.
Set d_sp=1-kappa^2 and

    T_sp=(exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d_sp, 0<kappa<1,
    T_sp=theta_sp/kappa,                         kappa>=1,
    H_sp=C[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]-1,
    A_sp=epsilon+(1+H_sp)/epsilon.                    (12)

For consecutive uncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new with difference
Delta, resolving the exact backward update along t(theta_old) gives

    t_old=B_D+A_D t_new,
    A_D=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B_D=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa.

Both are nonnegative. Differentiation gives
A_D'-(d_sp/kappa)A_D=-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0.
Thus A_D<=exp(d_sp Delta/kappa). If kappa<1, B_D<=Delta/kappa and
1+d_sp B_D<=exp(d_sp Delta/kappa); telescope to get t_j<=T_sp.
If kappa>=1, use A_D<=1 and B_D<=Delta/kappa to get the same bound
with its second definition. Consequently
y_j=R[(1+t_j)sin theta_j+kappa t_j cos theta_j]
<=C[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]. A cap leaves y unchanged, so

    0<=Q<=H_sp.                                      (13)

The exact maximum of q is

    qmax=1, kappa<=epsilon;
    qmax=sqrt((1+epsilon^2)/(1+kappa^2))
         exp(kappa[theta_sp-arctan(1/kappa)]), kappa>epsilon.

This follows from the sign of q' in(6). Define

    N_sp=max(0,kappa qmax-epsilon),
    B_sp=1+max(epsilon^(-2),N_sp/epsilon).              (14)

At every projected state y_j>=q_j, by(10), or by q_j=0 at a cap.
Also u_j-p_j>=-N_sp: a positive horizontal normal gives a
nonnegative displacement; with a negative normal, theta>arctan(1/kappa)
and p_j<=kappa qmax, whereas u_j>=epsilon. At a cap u_j>=C.

The new lower bound, in the direction needed for an upper bound on
normalized error, is particularly simple. Since R(theta)>=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)
and cos theta+sin theta>=1 on [0,pi/2],

    p(theta)+q(theta)>=1,
    P+Q>=s,
    (epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2
        >=(1+epsilon+P+Q)^2/2 >=(1+epsilon+s)^2/2.     (15)

The first quadratic inequality is the square of the elementary
Euclidean comparison (u+y)^2<=2(u^2+y^2). It uses actual P,Q,
not the upper bound P<=Cs. It imposes no sum-only dependence on them.

## 3. Local planes, their exact zero wedge, and smooth envelopes

Take a local selected step b>=1+epsilon^(-2), and any sigma>0 with

    sigma(P+epsilon b)<=1,
    sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.                 (16)

Set

    z_circ=(1-sigma(epsilon+P), sigma(1+Q))=(c_1,c_2),
    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b), Y=sigma Q,
    V={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_r},
    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V)<v,z-z_circ>.             (17)

The finite set retains the endpoint and cap slopes and every actual
backward projected slope. Each (p,-q) in it equals
[(p-epsilon q)/C](C,0)+q(epsilon,-1), with nonnegative coefficients.
Therefore its exact zero set is

    Z_phi={z: z_1<=c_1,
                 epsilon(z_1-c_1)-(z_2-c_2)<=0}.      (18)

Both endpoint constraints are necessary and the displayed conic
representation proves sufficiency for every other plane.

The function phi is nonnegative. From(16) and p>=epsilon q one has
<v,-z_circ><=0 for every v. It follows that phi=0 on z_1<=0,z_2>=0.
Also <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0, so
phi(U,y)=0 for y>=Y. These statements keep ALL local planes.

For any nonnegative finite maximum J of affine functions having a zero,
define env_1 J(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2]. The proximal minimizer is
unique by coercivity and strict convexity. If z is this point and
g=x-z, then g belongs to the convex subdifferential of J at z.
Conversely this subgradient condition proves proximal optimality by
expanding the squared norm. For another input and its pair z',g',
subgradient monotonicity gives ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>. A trial point
z gives the upper increment bound <g,w>+||w||^2/2; using z' and the
subgradient inequality at z gives the lower bound
<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2. These prove differentiability, convexity,
gradient g, and 1-Lipschitz continuity. Nonnegativity and attainment
show EXACTLY

    argmin(env_1 J)={J=0}, min(env_1 J)=0.              (19)

At x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j, let z_j=x_j-sigma v_j. The projection
inequalities proved in Section2 hold against every vector of V, hence
sigma v_j is an active subgradient of phi at z_j. Thus these are the
actual gradients of env_1 phi. The path starts at (1,0), follows
each actual ordinary alpha_j, then reaches (U+sigma epsilon b,Y)
with gradient sigma(epsilon,-1). Its selected step b reaches
(U,Y+sigma b). The first physical coordinate decreases and the second
increases, with x_(j),1>=U, 0<=x_(j),2<=Y during the ordinary block.

At the local proximal points, the more precise bounds are

    0<=z_(j),2=sigma(1+Q-y_j+q_j)<=sigma(1+Q),
    z_(j),1-U=sigma[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]>=0
                                               if b>=B_sp. (20)

The second bound pays N_sp through(14); the first uses y_j>=q_j,
including the overshooting arc. No error is dropped at a checkpoint.

## 4. First realization: full minimum set, rows, and exact radius

For any k>=1 selected checkpoints, write the actual word as
(block_0,b_0,block_1,...,b_(k-1),block_k), with block sums s_i.
Run Section2 separately on each ACTUAL full-step block_i, i<k.
Require b_i>=B_sp, and use

    M_i=P_i+max(epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon),
    sigma_i=1/M_i,
    D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1),
    ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i).                      (21)

Use (17) to define phi_i, c_(i,1),c_(i,2),U_i,Y_i and put

    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k,
    delta=D_k/(1+2s_k), Phi_k(z)=delta max(z_k-ell_k,0),
    J(z)=max_(0<=i<=k)Phi_i(z), f=env_1 J, x_0=e_0.    (22)

All D_i are positive. This is one function fixed before its run;
all old and future pieces remain in the maximum. Conditions(16) hold.
J is nonnegative, and J(0)=0 by its pre-activation zero wedges.
Equation(19) makes f convex and 1-smooth with minimum0.

At the start of block i the frozen coordinates are ell_j+D_j U_j
for j<i, the current coordinate is ell_i+D_i, and all later ones
are0. At each candidate local proximal point the current piece has
the local subgradient. The preceding piece is zero: its first input
is U_(i-1), and its second is at least ell_i/D_(i-1)>=Y_(i-1),
by(20). Earlier pieces stay in their zero wedges. The next piece
has first input at most0 because z_(i+1)<=ell_(i+1), by(20),
and second input0. Every further piece and the hinge are also zero.
Thus the current subgradient is a subgradient of J, even at a tie.
The local proximal point is the actual global proximal point.

After b_i the new coordinate equals D_i sigma_i(Q_i+b_i)
=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1), proving this state induction. In the tail, if u
is its already executed mass, the moving coordinate equals
ell_k+D_k-u delta. Its candidate proximal coordinate is at least
ell_k because D_k-(u+1)delta>=0 for 0<=u<=s_k. All old pieces are
zero there. Therefore the actual gradient is delta e_k throughout
the tail, including at the terminal point, and

    2f(x_n)=D_k^2/(1+2s_k).                            (23)

For ALL directed pairs of samples a,b, including the initial point,
all queried points, terminal point, and ANY minimizer, put
g_a=grad f(x_a), z_a=x_a-g_a. Then f_a=J(z_a)+||g_a||^2/2 and

    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2
        =J(z_a)-J(z_b)+<g_a,z_b-z_a><=0.               (24)

This proves every original smooth-convex interpolation row in its
specified orientation, without deleting optimum or terminal rows.
The actual Gram matrix is that of vectors in R^(k+1), hence is
positive semidefinite. Zero steps preserve identical actual jets.

The full minimizer set, directly from (18),(19),(22), is

    C_env={x: x_i<=zeta_i,
                 epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta_i-D_i c_(i,2), i<k;
                 x_k<=ell_k},
    zeta_i=ell_i+D_i c_(i,1).                          (25)

Define x_*=(c_(0,1),c_(0,2),0,...,0). The first component is at
its center. Its second coordinate equals ell_1, so the next component
has local input(0,0), and all remaining components are in their
pre-activation zero wedges; the hinge is zero. Thus x_* is feasible.
The vector e_0-x_* has the active-normal representation

    e_0-x_*=sigma_0[(P_0-epsilon Q_0)/C](C,0,0,...)
                  +sigma_0(1+Q_0)(epsilon,-1,0,...).

Both coefficients are nonnegative. For every x in C_env this implies
<e_0-x_*,x-x_*><=0. Expanding ||e_0-x||^2 proves that x_* is the
unique projection and gives

    rho_env^2=dist(e_0,C_env)^2
      =sigma_0^2[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2],           (26)
    E_env=(b_0-1)^2/([(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2](1+2s_k))
                     *product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/M_i]^2. (27)

In particular at k=1,

    E_env<=[2(b-1)^2]/[(1+s)^2(1+2t)],                (28)

where s,t are its actual prefix and suffix sums. This uses (15) as
a LOWER radius bound; it never uses P<=Cs to upper-bound payoff.

## 5. Second realization: full minimum set and nearest-distance structure

Use the same selection, but now require b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)).
Apply Section2 to the actual ordinary blocks with EVERY physical step
divided by2; P_i,Q_i in this section mean those HALF-step outputs.
In (17) use the local selected step beta_i=b_i/2 and scale

    sigma'_i=1/[Cs_i/2+epsilon b_i/2+A_sp].             (29)

Both conditions(16) follow from(11),(13). Define primed local objects
phi'_i,c'_(i,1),c'_(i,2),U'_i,Y'_i and f_i=env_1 phi'_i. Put

    G_i=sigma'_i b_i/2,
    D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,
    D'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i, ell'_(i+1)=D'_i sigma'_i Q_i,
    delta'=D'_k/(1+s_k).

Let Hub_d(z) be0 for z<=0, z^2/2 for 0<=z<=d, and
dz-d^2/2 for z>=d. The one static source-format function is

    F(x)=(1/2)sum_(i<k)(D'_i)^2
           f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)
               +(1/2)Hub_(delta')(x_k-ell'_k), x_0=e_0. (30)

It is nonnegative with F(0)=0. Every local term is convex and
1-smooth on its two coordinates. Their quadratic upper bounds,
after multiplication by1/2, count each coordinate at most twice,
including the hinge. Hence F(x+w)<=F(x)+<grad F(x),w>+||w||^2/2.
For a differentiable convex F this implies

    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>
                     >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.    (31)

Indeed apply that upper bound to V(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x and
w=-grad V(x); y minimizes V. Adding(31) and its exchanged version
proves 1-Lipschitz continuity of grad F. Taking x=x_b,y=x_a in(31)
is exactly Q_ab<=0 in(24)'s orientation for every original pair.
The optimum and terminal rows are included.

At the start of stage i, the frozen coordinates are
ell'_j+D'_j U'_j for j<i, current coordinate ell'_i+D'_i, and later
coordinates0. During its local PHYSICAL path, x_i>=ell'_i and
0<=x_(i+1)<=ell'_(i+1). Thus all old terms are in post-checkpoint
zero wedges and all future terms are in pre-activation zero wedges.
They have gradient0, since they are differentiable and nonnegative.
Only the current term acts; its coefficient1/2 turns each physical
step into the stipulated local half step. The selected step gives
next coordinate ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1). This proves the actual trajectory,
without using a horizontal proximal-coordinate bound. In the tail
the physical gradient is delta'/2; the final coordinate above ell'_k
is D'_k-delta' s_k/2>=delta'. Substitution yields

    2F(x_n)=(D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)].                       (32)

All original pieces, translations, scales, and the 1/2 coefficient
are retained. The specified origin is a minimizer at radius1, but
we now divide(32) by the ACTUAL closest distance.

Because each term is nonnegative and each envelope has zero set(18),
the complete minimum set is the nonempty closed polyhedron

    C_source={x: x_i<=zeta'_i,
                 epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta'_i-D'_i c'_(i,2), i<k;
                 x_k<=ell'_k},
    zeta'_i=ell'_i+D'_i c'_(i,1).                      (33)

It contains0. For k>=1, e_0 violates its first horizontal constraint,
so its squared distance rho_source^2 is positive and at most1.
Since C_source is a subset of the first local zero wedge, projection
onto that wedge by the normal argument of Section4 gives the genuine
lower bound

    rho_source^2 >= (sigma'_0)^2
                   [(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2].       (34)

In general this is a lower bound, NOT the exact distance. Unlike the
first realization, c'_(0,2)=sigma'_0(1+Q_0) is larger than
ell'_1=sigma'_0 Q_0. Later constraints must be checked.

For completeness an exact finite projection formula, with all those
constraints retained, is as follows. Let A_rad consist of rows
e_i^T and (epsilon e_i-e_(i+1))^T for i<k, followed by e_k^T,
and let t_rad consist of the right sides in(33). For each independent
row subset S, put A_S=(A_rad)_S, r_S=A_S e_0-(t_rad)_S,
K_S=A_S A_S^T and lambda_S=K_S^(-1)r_S. Include the empty subset
with value0. Then

    rho_source^2=max_(S independent,lambda_S>=0)
                          r_S^T K_S^(-1)r_S.          (35)

This is an expression, not an executed solver. For any feasible x and
lambda>=0 on S, completing a square gives
||e_0-x||^2>=2lambda^T r_S-||A_S^T lambda||^2. At lambda_S this is
the displayed entry, proving every such lower bound. At the actual
projection x_*, the normal e_0-x_* lies in the nonnegative cone of
its active constraint rows. To justify this without importing a
duality theorem, any direction having nonpositive inner products
with all active rows is feasible for a sufficiently small move;
projection optimality forces its inner product with e_0-x_* to be
nonpositive. A finitely generated cone is closed: remove linear
dependences from any nonnegative representation until independent
generators remain; it is a finite union of closed injective images
of orthants. If the normal were outside that cone, subtracting its
projection on the cone would contradict the preceding feasible-
direction inequality. Choose independent active rows representing
the normal. Activity gives r_S=K_S lambda, and their value in(35)
equals the squared distance. This proves equality.

## 6. Exact simplification with one source-format checkpoint

Suppose k=1, write s,t for the prefix and suffix sums, and omit
primes on the local sigma for this paragraph. P,Q are still the
HALF-step outputs of the whole ordered prefix. Formula(33) becomes

    x_0<=1-sigma(epsilon+P),
    epsilon x_0-x_1<=epsilon-sigma(epsilon^2+epsilon P+1+Q),
    x_1<=sigma Q.                                    (36)

Define E_0=P+epsilon+1/epsilon, a local abbreviation distinct from
the payoff E(f,h). The point

    x_*=(1-sigma E_0,sigma Q)                         (37)

satisfies all three constraints: the second and third are equalities,
and the first has slack sigma/epsilon. Its normal from e_0 is

    e_0-x_*=sigma(E_0,-Q)
       =sigma(E_0/epsilon)(epsilon,-1)
          +sigma(E_0/epsilon-Q)(0,1).                 (38)

The coefficients are positive because
E_0/epsilon-Q=(P/epsilon-Q)+1+epsilon^(-2)>0 by(11).
Thus(38), using the active second and third constraints, proves
projection by squared-distance expansion. We obtain the closed formula

    rho_source^2=sigma^2[(P+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2+Q^2],  (39)
    E_source=b^2/[8(1+t)((P+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2+Q^2)]. (40)

Both the first scale sigma and its activation allowance cancel
correctly, but the actual P,Q remain. This is an exact payoff of the
original function(30), not a new scale choice or a reset minimum.

By(15) for half steps, P+Q>=s/2. Since epsilon+1/epsilon>=5/2
on 0<epsilon<=1/2, the denominator in(39) obeys

    (P+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2+Q^2
       >=(P+Q+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2/2 >=(s+5)^2/8.

Consequently the actual one-checkpoint source payoff satisfies

    E_source<=b^2/[(1+t)(s+5)^2].                     (41)

This is a LOWER bound on its actual squared radius and an UPPER
bound on its actual payoff, with both directions justified.

## 7. All one-checkpoint selections on the small-tau hierarchy

For(2), put

    r_h=c(c-2), v_h=a/(c-2)=beta/r_h,
    M_j=1+S_j, d_j=a c^j, u_j=d_j/M_j.

The symbol r_h is a hierarchy constant, not a backward vector r_j.
With x=tau/m and gamma=4+2sqrt(2), expansion gives
r_h=1+gamma x+lambda^2 x^2>1>beta>0. Hence0<v_h<1. From the word
recurrence M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j and M_0=1, induction gives

    M_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,
    v_h c^j<=M_j<=c^j,
    a<=u_j<=c-2.                                     (42)

The simple constants needed uniformly on the assigned domain are

    c<121/50, 1/4<a<=u_j<21/50.                      (43)

Indeed sqrt(2)<283/200 by squaring, so lambda<483/200. Also
c<=lambda(1+1/6144)<484/200=121/50 because483<6144.
Moreover beta>=1-1/6144>3/4 and c<3, proving a>1/4.

Choose ANY occurrence of the sole checkpoint, at level j. Its value
is b=1+d_j. Its two immediate complete children are H_j, and neither
contains a selected checkpoint because this is the only selection.
Thus the ACTUAL prefix sum s and suffix sum t satisfy

    X=1+s>=M_j, Y=1+t>=M_j,
    S_m=s+b+t=X+Y+d_j-1.                             (44)

There may be arbitrarily much exterior mass on either side. No
assumption of root, first-occurrence, or record selection is used.

For the main realization, (28) and(44) give

    E_env/H_base(H_m)
      <=2d_j^2(2X+2Y+2d_j-1)/[X^2(2Y-1)].            (45)

For fixed Y,d_j this decreases with X>0: the derivative of
(2X+K)/X^2, where K=2Y+2d_j-1>0, is -2/X^2-2K/X^3.
For fixed X,d_j it decreases with Y>1/2 because the numerator
equals (2Y-1)+2X+2d_j. Hence(45) is at most

    2u_j^2(4M_j+2d_j-1)/(2M_j-1)
      =2u_j^2[2+u_j+(1+u_j)/(2M_j-1)].               (46)

Main eligibility implies b>=1+epsilon^(-2)>=5, so d_j>=4.
By(43), M_j=d_j/u_j>200/21>19/2, hence2M_j-1>18.
Using(43), the bracket in(46) is strictly less than

    121/50+(71/50)/18=2249/900<5/2.

Thus

    E_env/H_base(H_m)<5(21/50)^2=441/500.             (47)

All independent kappa,epsilon values, including kappa>1 with the
full N_sp threshold, have been included: their only use here was
actual eligibility and the universally valid displacement bound.

For the source realization, (41) gives

    E_source/H_base(H_m)
       <=(1+d_j)^2(2X+2Y+2d_j-1)/[Y(X+4)^2].         (48)

Source eligibility gives b>=2(1+epsilon^(-2))>=10, so d_j>=9.
For fixed X,d_j the ratio decreases with Y, since its varying part
is 2+(2X+2d_j-1)/Y. For fixed Y,d_j, differentiation of
(2X+K)/(X+4)^2 gives (8-2X-2K)/(X+4)^3<0, where
K=2Y+2d_j-1>=19. Thus(48) is at most

    [(1+d_j)/(M_j+4)]^2[4+2u_j-1/M_j].              (49)

Since u_j>1/4, we have (1+d_j)/(M_j+4)<=d_j/M_j=u_j.
Equations(43),(49) now imply

    E_source/H_base(H_m)<2u_j^2(2+u_j)
                 <5(21/50)^2=441/500.               (50)

Equations(47),(50) prove(3) for EVERY eligible checkpoint position
and EVERY parameter pair. No trajectory was changed during these
comparisons; no minimum-level deletion was applied to block outputs.

At k=0 the first function is the hinge envelope
Hub_(1/(1+2S_m)) from initial1, with zero set(-infinity,0]. Its
terminal payoff is 1/(1+2S_m). The second is
Hub_(1/(1+S_m))/2, with the same closest distance1 and payoff
1/[2(1+S_m)]<H_base(H_m). This proves(4).

## 8. Exact boundary tests and the failure of the multi-stage shortcut

First, an all-zero ordinary block has every r_j=(epsilon,-1),
every v_j=(epsilon,-1), and P=Q=0. The two one-checkpoint formulas
give respectively (b-1)^2/[(1+epsilon^2)(1+2t)] and
b^2/[8(1+t)(epsilon+1/epsilon)^2]. The radial bounds(15),(41)
remain valid. Such a prefix is not an eligible one-checkpoint
selection in H_(m,tau), whose complete child has positive mass at
every eligible level; the hierarchy proof does not assert otherwise.

For a single ordinary step alpha, whenever
alpha>=1+(1/kappa-epsilon)/C, condition(7) selects the cap, so

    P=C alpha, Q=0,
    ||r_0||^2=(epsilon+C alpha)^2+1.                  (51)

This is an actual arbitrarily huge-step test of(15), not a limiting
gradient-flow substitution. An equality threshold gives the same
projected point; zeros before a cap leave it capped.

Here is exact order dependence at a fixed total mass. Set
epsilon=kappa=1/2, theta_*=arctan(3/4), and

    R_*=C exp(-theta_*/2), p_*=(4/5)R_*, q_*=(3/5)R_*,
    A_*=1+2(1+q_*)/C.                                (52)

For a single last ordinary step1, the cap test fails, since1>1/4.
Equation(8) reduces to 2sin theta-(3/2)cos theta=0, whose root
is theta_*. Equivalently n(theta_*)=(1/2,-1), verifying the
support relation exactly. For chronological block(A_*,1), the
last step first gives r=(1/2+p_*,-1-q_*). The preceding A_* caps
because its right side in(7) is
1+q_*+1/4+p_*/2>1+q_*. Its ACTUAL outputs are therefore

    P=A_* C+p_*, Q=q_*>0.                            (53)

For chronological block(1,A_*), the last A_* already caps, since
A_*>1+2/C>1+3/(2C), and the preceding unit step stays capped.
Its outputs are instead

    P=(A_*+1)C, Q=0.                                 (54)

Both blocks have sum A_*+1, while p_*<C and q_*>0. Thus actual
P,Q are not functions of their sums. This test invalidates that
shortcut, not any formula for the unchanged ordered construction.

Finally test extension of the exact one-source-checkpoint radius(39).
Use TWO source checkpoints b_0=b_1=10, all three ordinary blocks
empty (or made of zeros), epsilon=kappa=1/2. Both checkpoints are
eligible exactly at the threshold. Then P_i=Q_i=0,

    sigma=1/(A_sp+5/2), 0<sigma<1/5,
    D'_1=5sigma, D'_2=25sigma^2, ell'_1=ell'_2=0.      (55)

The strict sigma bound follows from H_sp>0 and A_sp>5/2. The local
finite planes are exactly 0, (C,0), and (1/2,-1) about center
(1-sigma/2,sigma). The point

    x_*=(1-sigma/2,sigma,0)                           (56)

is at the first center. The second local input is(1/5,0). It is
inside its zero wedge: its horizontal constraint has positive slack,
and its endpoint plane evaluates, before the positive scale,

    (1/2)[1/5-(1-sigma/2)]+sigma
                    =-2/5+(5/4)sigma<-3/20<0.

The terminal hinge is zero. Thus(56) belongs to the full(33), with
every old/future plane retained. Its normal from e_0 is
sigma(1/2,-1,0), an active first endpoint normal. Projection follows,
and hence

    rho_source^2=(5/4)sigma^2.                        (57)

By contrast the proposed extrapolation of(39) would give
(25/4)sigma^2, five times the actual squared distance. The tail cap
x_1<=sigma Q_0 used in(36) is no longer a constraint; the genuine
tail cap is x_2<=0. This is the exact failed interface.

For additional trajectory evidence, the actual gradients along the
two selected steps of(30) are

    g_0=(sigma/4,-sigma/2,0),
    x_1=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma,0),
    g_1=(0,5sigma^2/4,-5sigma^2/2),
    x_2=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma-25sigma^2/2,25sigma^2).

At x_2 the local terms are zero and the tail value is
(D'_2)^2/4, so the actual closest payoff is250sigma^2, while
using the incorrect squared radius would give50sigma^2. Smoothness
and all interpolation rows follow directly from the static function
and(31). This is a boundary test outside H_(m,tau); no small-tau
escape or benchmark comparison is inferred from it.

## 9. Original benchmark and the finite small-tau deficit

Use ONLY accepted fact_id04219554e41d9750 for the following inputs.
For nonnegative u,v define

    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=i<n)Omega(C_i,C_(n-i-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=i<n)G(C_i,B_(n-i-1)).                  (58)

These are the original ALL-split denominators and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.
The accepted ray enclosure is

    lambda^(-j)B_(2^j-1)<=Z_1
           <=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(2^j))+3/100)^2,       (59)

where Z_1 is their positive finite limit. The same accepted fact's
Section8 certifies every entry of its fixed public interval table
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA2562a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
Only its header, row1024, and hash were read in this round; no
generator, recurrence, checker, or mathematical evaluation was run.
With D80=1208925819614629174706176, that row gives

    D80 B_1024<=19229705732238461978760749948
                  <15910 D80
                   =19234009790068750169575260160.    (60)

Since19600<19602, sqrt(2)>140/99. The binomial identity
lambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2) implies lambda^10>6725, because

sqrt(15910)<127 give

    Z_1<(15910+381/50+9/10000)/6725
         <15918/6725<237/100.                        (61)

The last integer comparison is1591800<1593825. All these are written
scalar deductions from the existing accepted endpoint, not a new
benchmark evaluation and not a claim that the old draft is a fact.

For the hierarchy, (42) gives

    (1+2S_m)/lambda^m
      =2v_h(1+tau/m)^m+2(1-v_h)(2/lambda)^m-lambda^(-m). (62)

Also r_h-beta=(5+2sqrt(2))tau/m+lambda^2 tau^2/m^2
<8tau/m+6tau^2/m^2<9/(4m) for the stated m,tau. Since r_h>1,

    v_h=beta/r_h>1-9/(4m)>255/256,
    lambda^(-m)<1/256.                               (63)

For m>=2, binomial truncation gives
(1+tau/m)^m>=1+tau+tau^2/4. At tau=1/4 this is81/64, so(62),(63)
imply (1+2S_m)/lambda^m>20623/8192>5/2. Uniformly for
1/5<=tau<=1/4 the lower bound121/100 instead gives
(1+2S_m)/lambda^m>6161/2560>12/5. Combining(59),(61) with(4)
proves precisely(5), for EVERY m>=1536. No choice of benchmark
optimizer or alternate recurrence has been substituted.

For fixed tau>0, v_h tends1, (1+tau/m)^m tends exp(tau), and
(2/lambda)^m tends0. Hence(62) tends2exp(tau). The accepted ray
limit(59) proves the stated limit for L_actual,<=1. It is a limit
for this delimited actual family, never an upper bound on W_n.

## 10. Remaining interface, scope, and record

The endpoint projection mechanism succeeds for all actual one-checkpoint
realizations, with a uniform strict factor(3). It fully pays the true
initial distance, both half-step and full-step trajectories, every
affine plane, the terminal value, and every directed interpolation row.
It uses no backward-state replacement and does not collapse ordered
blocks to sums; sums enter only through inequalities proved from each
actual projected slope.

The same mechanism has NOT proved an all-count family obstruction.
For the main realization the exact remaining expression is(27), with
each M_i computed from its actual ordered block. For the source
realization it is (D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)rho_source^2] with(33)--(35).
The first-wedge lower bound(34) is valid but weaker than the special
one-stage formula(39); the explicit test(55)--(57) refutes copying
that stronger formula when a further stage replaces the first tail.
The exact equal-sum test(52)--(54) also bars replacing actual P_i,Q_i
after deleting a checkpoint without a new comparison proof.

Thus the unresolved task is to control the product of ACTUAL transfer
denominators together with the first-block or chained source distance
when k>=2 on the same H_(m,tau) family. There is no claimed counterexample
to its all-count comparison, no normalized escape, no conclusion about
the unrestricted W_n, and no original-target solution.

Matlas was queried for the minimizer/projection interface and the
endpoint-displacement bound; its results were inapplicable. Web search
provided generic Moreau-envelope orientation. No external theorem is
imported, and no new technical literature branch is claimed: the
explicit finite-plane and Euclidean arguments above are self-contained.
The skills used were query-memory, direct-proving, search-math-results,
and construct-counterexamples. The assignment closes the formal gate,
so this complete joint scoped proof remains UNVERIFIED. This is the
end of the ONE allocated analytical mechanism/round, without renewal.

Two exact boundary counterexamples for extending max2's one-checkpoint projection argument. Status: joint UNVERIFIED mathematics; these are not accepted facts. The complete common construction and proofs are also included in172014083da57ddf, but the explicit tests are given here.

1. A false all-count radius inequality.
Set epsilon=kappa=1/2, theta_sp=arctan2, C=(sqrt5/2)exp(theta_sp/2), T=(4/3)(exp(3theta_sp/2)-1), H=C(1+3T/2)-1 and A=1/2+2(1+H)>5/2. Let sigma=1/(A+5/2), so0<sigma<1/5. Define the finite convex maximum on R^2
phi(z)=sigma max{0, C(z_0-1+sigma/2), (z_0-1+sigma/2)/2-z_1+sigma},
and f_loc(x)=min_z[phi(z)+||x-z||^2/2].
For d>0 let Hub_d(z)=0 if z<=0,z^2/2 if0<=z<=d,and dz-d^2/2 ifz>=d.
On R^3 define the STATIC function
F(x)=f_loc(x_0,x_1)/2+(25sigma^2/2)f_loc(x_1/(5sigma),x_2/(5sigma))+Hub_(25sigma^2)(x_2)/2.
This is exactly the source construction with physical word(10,10), BOTH steps selected, all ordinary blocks empty, D'_0=1,D'_1=5sigma,D'_2=25sigma^2,ell'_i=0 and actual P_i=Q_i=0. Each checkpoint meets b=2(1+epsilon^-2)=10. No constant or curve is retuned.

The local zero wedge is z_0<=1-sigma/2 and (z_0-1+sigma/2)/2-z_1+sigma<=0: both displayed endpoint planes are necessary and sufficient. A nonnegative envelope has the same zero set because its defining quadratic and phi are nonnegative and attain their infimum. Therefore the full minimum set of F is the intersection of BOTH scaled wedges and x_2<=0; its minimum is0 and it contains0.

The point x_*=(1-sigma/2,sigma,0) is at the first center. Its second local input is(1/5,0); the second endpoint plane there is -2/5+5sigma/4<-3/20, and the horizontal plane is also negative. The tail is0. Thus x_* is a minimizer of the FULL function. The normal e_0-x_*=sigma(1/2,-1,0) is the active first endpoint normal. Every minimizer x therefore satisfies <e_0-x_*,x-x_*><=0. Expanding ||e_0-x||^2 proves
dist(e_0,argmin F)^2=5sigma^2/4.
The proposed all-count lower bound sigma^2[(P_0+epsilon+epsilon^-1)^2+Q_0^2] would be25sigma^2/4 and is FALSE by a factor of five. In a genuine one-stage function the tail constrains x_1<=sigma Q_0; here the genuine tail constrains x_2<=0, and the first constraint is absent.

For actual trajectory evidence, from e_0 the gradients at the two queried points are
g_0=(sigma/4,-sigma/2,0),
x_1=e_0-10g_0=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma,0),
g_1=(0,5sigma^2/4,-5sigma^2/2),
x_2=x_1-10g_1=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma-25sigma^2/2,25sigma^2).
These gradients follow from the proximal point equal to the local center at local input(1,0); sigma(1/2,-1) is an active subgradient. At each query all other local terms vanish. At x_2 both local terms vanish and F(x_2)=(25sigma^2)^2/4. Its TRUE payoff is250sigma^2, whereas the false radius would give50sigma^2.

For smoothness: if z=prox_phi(x),g=x-z, subgradient monotonicity yields ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>; comparison of the minimizing quadratics gives gradient g and the 1-smooth upper inequality. In F, the coefficients1/2 count each coordinate at most twice, proving the 1-smooth upper inequality for F. Applied to F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x with move -grad F(x)+grad F(y), it gives F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2. Hence EVERY directed original interpolation row, including the terminal point and minimizers, holds. This is a genuine static convex 1-smooth test, not independent local jets. It is OUTSIDE the H_(m,tau) family and proves NO escape on that family.

2. Equal ordinary sums do not fix actual backward outputs.
Keep epsilon=kappa=1/2 and the same C. Let theta_*=arctan(3/4), R_*=C exp(-theta_*/2), p_*=4R_*/5,q_*=3R_*/5 and A_*=1+2(1+q_*)/C. The backward endpoint is(1/2,-1). For a final ordinary step1, the cap condition1<=kappa[epsilon+(1-1)C]=1/4 fails. The uncapped equation <(1/2,-1),e(theta)-2t(theta)>/R(theta)=0 reduces to2sin theta-(3/2)cos theta=0, giving precisely theta_*; its normal n(theta_*)=(1/2,-1) supports the spiral arc since exp(-kappa delta)(cos delta+kappa sin delta) is maximized atdelta0. The new state is(1/2+p_*,-1-q_*).

For chronological block(A_*,1), the preceding A_* caps, because the cap right side is1+q_*+1/4+p_*/2>1+q_*. Thus its actual outputs are P=A_*C+p_*,Q=q_*>0. For chronological block(1,A_*), its last step already caps since A_*>1+3/(2C); the earlier unit step stays capped. Its outputs are P=(A_*+1)C,Q=0. Both sums equal A_*+1 but their outputs differ, with p_*<C. This refutes a sum-only output or deletion shortcut. It does not refute the proved lower bound P+Q>=sum alpha, which follows individually from every arc slope p+q>=1, and it does not refute the actual full construction.

These tests support only the explicit failed extrapolations just stated. The all-count actual-family comparison on small tau and the original exact-profile goal remain OPEN. No mathematical program, solver, numerical evaluation, or fact_submit was used.

# R11 handoff: true-input cancellation completed; child surplus cannot be discarded

**UNVERIFIED draft mathematics. Original Goal unchanged and OPEN. No all-depth U certificate or original-target proof/disproof was obtained.**

Grant `233b55a4bc4f693c` is complete. One native invocation; two analytical approaches; mathematical programs/arrays/solvers, formal submissions, additional agents and additional models: all zero. Parent owns ledgers and shared administration. Do not renew this grant automatically.

## What the complete calculation says

The current direct target remains `W(H_m)<=17/[16(1+2S_m)]` for every sufficiently large enclosing m, on the full original class with `W=2R`, original selected minimizers and original radii. The literal hierarchy retains `epsilon=1/(4m)`, `beta=1-epsilon`, `c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon)`, `a=beta/c`, `H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j)` with the same parameters in every child. Accepted facts `931b9653ce0f7201` and `1e0bf3f41457d8e4` retain only their benchmark/transfer/H3 scopes.

Approach 1 keeps both actual input projections `p0=P(x/M)` and `ell=P(x/A)`. The left-query fan cancels at the SAME retained fraction eta, and every theta-dependent finite-vector term cancels too. For `chi=1-eta`, `Delta=alpha-b`, `R0=M(p0-r)-A(ell-r)`, the complete left finite contribution is

`chi[||R0||²-Delta²||p-r||²-2Delta sigma h<r,p-r>-2A(M-A)<r,u>]`.

The independent parent-B transport is

`N0(q)=N0(r)+N_R,q+<eN,q-r>`, `eN=nR-n0`.

Subtracting the same `gamma B_parent` is compatible with all input-normal coefficients whenever `gamma<=chi(1-A/M)`. Here `M>=2A`, so `gamma<=chi/2` works uniformly. This includes `eta=3/4,gamma=1/1200`. The negative root normal is exactly converted using the actual bridge relation; it is never dropped. PROOF_ATTEMPT equations (2.7)--(2.9) give the complete identity, including the parent square, both discount squares, right-child quadratic, endpoint transport and every remaining normal.

Writing actual child surplus as `F=E-eta R-gamma B`, that identity is

`M F_parent=A F_left+M F_right+Q+N`.

It is false that one may discard BOTH child surpluses and prove `Q+N>=0` on all actual inherited data. This is now an actual-data obstruction, not just an indefinite free-vector coefficient matrix.

## Actual arbitrarily deep failure of that payment rule

Use one fixed compact body `K=[-1,1]`, trailing d=1 and fixed `kappa=16/17`. Let `c0=1+sqrt(2)`, `a0=1/c0`. At the limiting epsilon=0 words,

`S_j=c0^j-1`, `k_j=a0(S_j+1)`, and the actual clipped-gradient map obeys `T_Hj(k_j)=(-1)^j a0` by a direct induction.

For every even j start the parent H_(j+1) at `x=S_j+1`. The left child consists entirely of saturated positive queries and ends at `p=1`. The bridge gives `w=-k_j`; the actual right child ends at `q=-a0`. Both input auxiliaries and the right auxiliary lie inside K; their normals and the bridge normal are zero. Thus every normal in the completed payment vanishes.

For `s=S_j`, `A=1+2kappa s`, `sigma=2kappa-1`, `delta=1-kappa`, the actual child quantities are

`R_L=B_L=sigma² s²/A`,

`E_L-R_L=2delta s`,

`F_L={[1-(eta+gamma)sigma²]s²+2delta s}/A`,

`F_R=a0² F_L`.

The complete discarded-surplus expression has the exact asymptotic sign

`Q/s² -> -a0(1-eta sigma²)-gamma[(1+2kappa)²-sqrt(2)sigma²] < 0`.

This sign is strict for every `0<=eta<=1`, `gamma>=0`, and `16/17<=kappa<1`. For the chosen leaf coefficients, both child and parent invariant surpluses are nevertheless positive for sufficiently large j; specifically `M F_parent/s² -> 1-eta sigma²-gamma(1+2kappa)² > 0`. The invariant itself is therefore NOT refuted by this example.

To obtain literal allowed inherited examples, fix any sufficiently large even j and use the actual start `x=S_j(m)+1`, actual bridge `-k_j(m)`, and actual endpoint `y_m=T_Hj(m)(-k_j(m))`. Every physical sample varies continuously as m tends to infinity. The left endpoint remains exactly 1, and the right endpoint tends to `-a0`. All the displayed auxiliary normals remain zero for sufficiently large m. The strict negative Q persists, with `m>=max(1536,j+1)`. Since j is unbounded, this is an arbitrarily deep inherited obstruction, not a fixed-horizon screen. No child parameter is reset.

The exact defect is the order-s child surplus that becomes order s squared after multiplication by A or M. It includes the real discount term and the unused canonical fraction. A mere change of the common eta/gamma does not fix this discarded-surplus scheme.

# R11: complete two-input transport and an actual obstruction to discarding child surplus

**All new mathematics in this packet is UNVERIFIED under Danus policy. The original minimax problem remains OPEN. This is not an all-depth upper certificate.**

Grant `233b55a4bc4f693c`: one native analytical invocation, at most two substantive approaches. Exactly two approaches were used. Mathematical programs, numerical arrays, solvers, formal submissions, additional agents and additional model invocations: zero. The parent owns the ledger and all shared administration.

The two true input projections remove the R10 expanding-normal-fan argument. Completing that transport, including the parent endpoint remainder, does not close the induction. There is an actual, arbitrarily deep inherited family on the fixed body `[-1,1]` where the complete lower expression obtained by replacing both child gaps by the proposed common two-remainder bound is strictly negative. All normals retained in that expression vanish on this family. This is a counterexample to that particular payment rule, not to the two-remainder invariant or to the desired U inequality. The positive child surplus discarded by that rule is explicitly computed below.

The second approach retains the entire signed child energy and the actual cross-subtree normal rows. Its exact recurrence is derived for every trailing parameter. It supplies no quantitative uniform lower bound on the signed right-child energy, so it does not yield a final coefficient `kappa_m >= 16/17`. No complete target-level candidate exists for a finished-object independent adversarial audit.

## 1. Fixed target and notation

The unchanged objective is to determine whether

`lim_(n->infinity) W_n^*/U_n^ZJ = 1`,

where `W_n(h)` is the supremum of `2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2)` over every finite dimension, every convex differentiable L-smooth function with an attained minimum, every original selected minimizer and distinct original start, under the literal predetermined nonnegative word `h`. The infimum defining `W_n^*` is over all such words, including words with zero entries. `W=2R`; `U_n^ZJ=1/B_n` uses the original all-integer-split Zhang--Jiang benchmark.

Fix one enclosing integer `m >= 1536`, with

\[
\epsilon=\frac1{4m},\quad \beta=1-\epsilon,\quad
c=(1+\sqrt2)(1+\epsilon),\quad a=\beta/c,
\quad H_0=(),\quad H_{j+1}=(H_j,1+ac^j,H_j).
\]

Every child uses these same parameters. Write `S_j=sum H_j` and `D_m=1+2S_m`. Accepted fact `931b9653ce0f7201` gives `B_(2^m-1)/D_m < 47/50`. Its conditional separation follows if the missing full-class upper bound `W(H_m)<=17/(16D_m)` holds for every sufficiently large enclosing m. Neither that premise nor a substitute is proved here.

Fix one arbitrary nonempty compact convex `K` containing zero. All samples use `P=P_K`. Set

\[
U_d(x)=\max_{g\in K}\{2\langle x,g\rangle-d\|g\|^2\}.
\]

If `r=P(x/D)`, `n=x-Dr`, and `z in K`, then

\[
\operatorname{Rem}(x,D,z)
=D\|r-z\|^2+2\langle n,r-z\rangle.
\tag{1.1}
\]

The normal convention throughout is `<n,r-z> >= 0`. For an actual word of mass `s`, input x, output y, and trailing `d>=1`, define

\[
D=d+2\kappa s,\quad q=P(y/d),\quad r=P(x/D),
\quad z=q+\frac{x-y-sq}{D},
\]
\[
E=U_D(x)-U_d(y),\quad R=\operatorname{Rem}(x,D,z),
\quad B=\operatorname{Rem}(x,D,q).
\tag{1.2}
\]

Here `16/17<=kappa<=1`, `delta=1-kappa`, `sigma=2kappa-1`. The mixture z is feasible because all physical steps are nonnegative and `D-s>0`.

The proposed sufficient induction is

\[
E_j\ge\eta R_j+\gamma B_j\qquad(d\ge1),
\tag{1.3}
\]

with common `eta=3/4`, `gamma=1/1200`, and `kappa=16/17`. R10 proves this leaf base in draft. No higher instance of (1.3) is assumed as a fact. Below, child instances are explicit conditional premises used to test preservation. Denote the actual surplus by

\[
F_j=E_j-\eta R_j-\gamma B_j.
\tag{1.4}
\]

Accepted `1e0bf3f41457d8e4` supplies the finite-word full-supremum projection transfer and the actual same-parameter H3 law, not higher primitive positivity. An all-body law `U_1(T_Hm x)<=U_(1+(32/17)S_m)(x)` would retain every original minimizer and radius, since for any polar-cone minimizer `x_star`,

\[
U_D(x)\le\frac{\|x-x_\star\|^2}{D},\qquad
\frac1{1+(32/17)S_m}\le\frac{17}{16D_m}.
\]

This remains a sufficient implication with an unproved premise.

## 2. Approach 1: finish the common-fraction transport

### 2.1 Actual parent data and retained radial relations

At a parent with two H_j children, let

\[
s=S_j,\quad k=ac^j,\quad b=1+k,\quad h=s+b,
\quad C=1+s,\quad S=s+h,
\]
\[
A=1+2\kappa s,\quad\alpha=A-s,\quad
D=d+2\kappa s,\quad M=D+2\kappa h,\quad\Delta=\alpha-b.
\]

The actual path is `x -> v`, `p=P(v)`, `w=v-bp`, then `w -> y`. Set

\[
G=x-v,\quad \ell=P(x/A),\quad n_L=x-A\ell,
\quad u=\ell-\frac{G+\alpha p}{A},
\]
\[
p_0=P(x/M),\quad n_0=x-Mp_0,\quad w_0=Mp_0-A\ell,
\]
\[
r=P(w/D),\quad n_R=w-Dr,\quad q=P(y/d),\quad
u_R=r-\frac{w-y+(D-s)q}{D}.
\]

The left child uses trailing 1 and the right child trailing d. Define `R_L,B_L,E_L` and `R_R,B_R,E_R` by (1.2). The parent uses `R_all,B_all,Psi` at mass S and trailing d. Let

\[
e=p-r,\quad z=q-r,\quad
e_N=n_R-n_0=w_0+Au+\Delta p-Dr,
\]
\[
R_0=M(p_0-r)-A(\ell-r),\qquad \chi=1-\eta.
\tag{2.1}
\]

Every projection or mixture point just used is actual; the displayed vector differences retain their defining relations. In particular, with `N_0(t)=<n_0,p_0-t>`, the two input projections obey

\[
N_0(\ell)+\langle n_L,\ell-p_0\rangle
=\langle w_0,\ell-p_0\rangle\ge0.
\tag{2.2}
\]

Writing `zeta=ell-p0`, this includes the radial constraint

\[
A\|\zeta\|^2\le(M-A)\langle p_0,\zeta\rangle.
\]

No vector below is freed from these relations.

### 2.2 The fan cancellation and complete disappearance of theta

For `0<=theta<=min(1,b/alpha)`, use the parent note's feasible mixture

\[
z_\theta=
\frac{(1-\theta)G+(b-\theta\alpha)p+(M-h)r+\theta A\ell}{M}.
\]

The coefficient of the residual left-normal term after subtracting the same parent canonical fraction is `-2chi(1-theta)A<n_L,u>`. The parent's true normal rows satisfy

\[
A\langle n_L,u\rangle
=\sum_{i\in L}h_iN_0(g_i)+\alpha N_0(p)-A N_0(\ell)
 +A\langle w_0,u\rangle.
\tag{2.3}
\]

Thus the complete normal fan over the original left queries cancels. Using

\[
N_0(p)-N_0(r)=N_r-\langle e_N,r-p\rangle,
\quad N_r=\langle n_R,r-p\rangle,
\]

leaves the nonnegative input rows

`2chi[A N_0(ell)+(M-A)N_0(r)]`,

a root normal contribution `-2chi Delta N_r`, and finite-vector terms.

Here is the full square cancellation. Put `V=w0+Delta p-Dr`. Then

\[
M(p_0-z_\theta)=V-\sigma h r+(1-\theta)Au.
\]

Combining the parent square, the left child square, all u cross terms, and the finite term from (2.3) gives

\[
\begin{aligned}
&\chi\|V-\sigma hr+(1-\theta)Au\|^2
-\chi(1-\theta)^2A^2\|u\|^2\\
&\quad+2\chi\theta A\langle\Delta p-(D+\sigma h)r,u\rangle
-2\chi(1-\theta)A\langle w_0,u\rangle
 +2\chi\Delta\langle V+Au,r-p\rangle\\
&=\chi\left[
\|R_0\|^2-\Delta^2\|e\|^2
-2\Delta\sigma h\langle r,e\rangle
-2A(M-A)\langle r,u\rangle\right].
\tag{2.4}
\end{aligned}
\]

For the last step use `D+sigma h-Delta=M-A`. All theta dependence disappears. In particular, optimizing theta cannot repair the remaining expression after the same child fraction is inserted in this way.

### 2.3 Independent transport of the parent's endpoint remainder

The endpoint comparison needs its own accounting:

\[
B_{\rm all}=M\|p_0-q\|^2+2N_0(q),
\]
\[
N_0(q)=N_0(r)+N_{R,q}+\langle e_N,q-r\rangle,
\quad N_{R,q}=\langle n_R,r-q\rangle.
\tag{2.5}
\]

Subtracting `gamma M B_all` therefore costs

\[
\gamma M^2\|p_0-q\|^2+2\gamma M\langle e_N,z\rangle
 +2\gamma M N_0(r)+2\gamma M N_{R,q}.
\]

The last normal term exactly cancels the `2gamma M N_R,q` in the R10 right-child payment. The initial-normal coefficient stays nonnegative provided

\[
\gamma\le\chi(1-A/M).
\tag{2.6}
\]

This condition is uniform here. Since `h=s+b>=s+1=C` and `2kappa>=1`,

`2kappa h >= 2kappa C >= A` and `M=A+(d-1)+2kappa h>=2A`.

Consequently `gamma<=chi/2` suffices. The actual leaf constants obey it: `1/1200<1/8`. This establishes compatibility of the endpoint NORMAL coefficients only; it establishes no endpoint quadratic bound.

### 2.4 Exact full transport, with both child surpluses visible

Let

\[
N_p=\langle v-p,p-r\rangle,\quad
K_p=h\left(1+\frac C{D+k}\right),\quad
K_r=\frac{hC}{D+k}-\chi\Delta,
\]
\[
\Theta=\frac{h[CD-k(C+D+k)]}{D+k},\qquad
\mathrm{Fan}_R=\sum_{i\in R}h_i\langle n_R,r-g_i\rangle.

Then the complete identity is

M F_P=A F_L+M F_R+\mathcal Q+\mathcal N,
\tag{2.7}
\]

where

+\gamma A B_L+\Theta\|e\|^2\\
&+2\delta\bigl(Mh\|r\|^2-As\|p\|^2\bigr)\\
&+\eta\bigl[
2\kappa hD\|u_R\|^2+2D\sigma h\langle u_R,q\rangle

and

+2\chi A N_0(\ell)
+2[\chi(M-A)-\gamma M]N_0(r)\\
&+\frac{4\eta\kappa h}{D}\mathrm{Fan}_R
+\frac{2\eta hd}{D}N_{R,q}.

\]

The actual nonnegative quantity `gamma A B_L` is left intact in (2.8), including its normal part. The negative left discount square `-2delta As||p||^2` is present.

To check the right quadratic in (2.8), let `t0=-D u_R+sigma h(q-r)`. The R10 payment contributes `eta MD||u_R||^2+gamma MD||z||^2`; the remaining canonical transport contributes `-eta||t0||^2-2eta sigma h<r,t0>`. Expanding gives the displayed right bracket. The right normal coefficients in (2.9) use exactly

`(M-D)(D-s)/D-sigma h=hd/D`.

If `K_r<0`, the negative root coefficient is not discarded. The actual bridge identity is

\[
N_p+N_r=(D+k)\langle p,e\rangle-D\|e\|^2.
\]

Move `2K_r[(D+k)<p,e>-D||e||^2]` into `Q` and replace its root normal part by `2(h+chi Delta)N_p`. This coefficient is positive: for `Delta<0`, `h+chi Delta>=h+Delta=s+alpha>0`; the other case is immediate. Equations (2.7)--(2.9), with this optional exact rewrite, contain no uncontrolled independent negative normal.

The direct preservation rule would now use only `F_L,F_R>=0` and prove `Q+N>=0` on every actual datum. That last claim is false, as follows.

## 3. An arbitrarily deep actual obstruction to that payment rule

### 3.1 Exact limiting trajectories on one compact body

Use `K=[-1,1]`, so `P(t)=max(-1,min(1,t))`. Write

\[
c_0=1+\sqrt2,\quad a_0=1/c_0=\sqrt2-1.
\]

First consider the limiting parameters `epsilon=0`, only to construct a strict inequality that will subsequently be transferred to finite allowed enclosing m. Superscript zero indicates these limiting words. Their exact masses and bridges satisfy

\[
S_j^0=c_0^j-1,\quad C_j^0=c_0^j,\quad k_j^0=a_0 C_j^0.
\tag{3.1}
\]

The actual scalar map is odd, and

\[
T_{H_j^0}(k_j^0)=(-1)^j a_0.
\tag{3.2}
\]

For j=1 this is the step `sqrt(2)` from 1. For the induction, `k_j^0=C_(j-1)^0=S_(j-1)^0+1`. Every query of the first H_(j-1) child is at least 1, and that child ends at 1. Its bridge sends 1 to `-k_(j-1)^0`. Oddness and the induction hypothesis prove (3.2).

Take any even j, and the parent H_(j+1)^0 with start

`x=C=s+1`, `s=S_j^0`, and trailing `d=1`.

Its left child has every gradient equal to 1 and ends at `v=p=1`. Its bridge sends this to `w=-k=-a0 C`. Its right child ends at

`y=q=-a0`.

All relevant auxiliaries are the true interior projections

\[
\ell=C/A,\quad p_0=C/M,\quad r=-a_0C/A,
\quad D=A,
\quad S=c_0C-1.
\tag{3.3}
\]

Thus `n_L=n_0=n_R=v-p=0`. Every normal in (2.9) vanishes; so do the optional bridge rewrite and all the radial normal rows. These are actual data from the one fixed body, not independently chosen vectors or normal values.

### 3.2 Exact child surplus and exact parent deficit

For the left child,

\[
R_L=B_L=\frac{\sigma^2s^2}{A},\quad
E_L=\frac{s^2+2\delta s}{A},\quad
E_L-R_L=2\delta s.
\tag{3.4}
\]

The equality in the last expression uses `1-sigma^2=4kappa delta`. Hence

\[
F_L=\frac{[1-(\eta+\gamma)\sigma^2]s^2+2\delta s}{A}.
\tag{3.5}
\]

The actual right child has

\[
R_R=B_R=a_0^2 R_L,\quad E_R=a_0^2 E_L,
\quad F_R=a_0^2F_L.
\tag{3.6}
\]

For example, its comparison mixture equals q exactly, because `w=(s+1)q`; this checks `R_R=B_R` without a norm estimate.

The actual parent satisfies

\[
M F_P=C^2-Ma_0^2-\eta\sigma^2S^2a_0^2
-\gamma(C+Ma_0)^2.
\tag{3.7}
\]

Since all normal terms vanish, (2.7) gives the following exact value for the completed payment expression:

\[
\mathcal Q=
C^2-Ma_0^2-\eta\sigma^2S^2a_0^2-\gamma(C+Ma_0)^2
-(A+Ma_0^2)F_L.
\tag{3.8}
\]

As even j tends to infinity, `C/s->1`, `S/s->c0`, and `c0 a0=1`. Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\frac{\mathcal Q}{s^2}\longrightarrow
-a_0(1-\eta\sigma^2)
-\gamma\bigl[(1+2\kappa)^2-\sqrt2\,\sigma^2\bigr].}
\tag{3.9}
\]

For any fixed `kappa in [16/17,1)`, `0<=eta<=1`, and `gamma>=0`, this limit is strictly negative. Indeed `sigma^2<1`, and the second bracket is positive: `(1+2kappa)^2>=4` while `sqrt(2)sigma^2<=sqrt(2)<2`. Thus even choosing another common retained fraction cannot make this same discarded-surplus payment work at a discounted kappa.

For the selected constants the children satisfy their proposed invariant strictly on this family: (3.5) is positive. The parent invariant itself also holds for all sufficiently large j, because

\[
\frac{M F_P}{s^2}\longrightarrow
1-\eta\sigma^2-\gamma(1+2\kappa)^2
\ge\frac14-\frac9{1200}>0.
\tag{3.10}
\]

The negative quantity is solely the remainder after BOTH positive child surpluses have been discarded.

### 3.3 Transfer of the strict failure to literal inherited parameters

This construction is not confined to an inadmissible limiting word. Fix any sufficiently large even j for which (3.8) is strictly negative. For every finite allowed enclosing m use the actual parameters prescribed in Section 1 and the actual start

`x=S_j(m)+1`.

All left-child physical queries are at least 1, so the left child still ends exactly at `p=v=1`. The bridge starts the actual right child at `-k_j(m)`. Define, rather than assign,

`y_m=T_(H_j(m))(-k_j(m))`.

Projection onto the fixed interval is continuous, and the finite word's steps are continuous in epsilon. Consequently, as m tends to infinity with this j fixed,

`y_m -> -a0`, together with convergence of every physical and auxiliary sample. For all sufficiently large m, `|y_m|<1`, so `q=y_m=P(y_m)`, and all auxiliary projections in (3.3) remain interior. Every normal retained in (2.9) still vanishes. The complete expression (2.8) is continuous in these actual data, so it remains strictly negative.

Choose such an enclosing m also larger than `max(1536,j+1)`. This is a literal internal H_(j+1) of the requested hierarchy, with no child parameter reset. Since even j can be arbitrarily large, this gives arbitrarily deep actual inherited failures of the direct payment rule. This is an analytic continuity argument with a strict limiting sign, not a finite numerical screen or a solver-generated example.

What it rules out precisely is:

> Replace both actual child gaps by exactly `eta R+gamma B`, discard the entire nonnegative child surplus, and prove the remaining expression is nonnegative by the retained actual radial and bridge relations.

That assertion fails on actual data. It does not rule out retaining quantitative child surplus, changing the joint quantity, using additional original-query inequalities, proving the U law directly, or disproving the original minimax conjecture by another family.

## 4. Approach 2: keep the complete signed energies and original cross-subtree rows

The lost surplus in (3.4) is order s, and its contribution to (2.7) is order s squared. Treating it as an arbitrarily small numerical correction cannot repair the recurrence. The second approach keeps the full quantities rather than discarding them.

For a child or parent define the actual signed energy

\[
V_d(x,y;s)=\|x\|^2-\|y+sP(y/d)\|^2.
\tag{4.1}
\]

It satisfies the exact identity

\[
E=R+\frac{V_d}{d+2\kappa s}+2\delta s\|P(y/d)\|^2.
\tag{4.2}
\]

No positivity of `V_d` at higher children is assumed. In particular, (4.2) is not an all-level terminal-norm reserve assertion.

At the same actual parent put

\[
V_L=\|x\|^2-\|v+sp\|^2,\quad
V_R=\|w\|^2-\|y+sq\|^2,\quad
V_P=\|x\|^2-\|y+Sq\|^2.
\]

Use the physical bridge normal `n_p=v-p` and the actual terminal auxiliary normal `n_q=y-dq`. If the right-child gradients are `g_i`, define the original-query sums

\[
\mathcal A_p=\sum_{i\in R}h_i\langle n_p,p-g_i\rangle,\qquad
\mathcal A_q=\sum_{i\in R}h_i\langle n_q,q-g_i\rangle,
\]
\[
I_p=\langle n_p,p-q\rangle,\qquad
I_q=\langle n_q,q-p\rangle.
\tag{4.3}
\]

All four are nonnegative on the original common body. Every right query appears with its actual step weight.

Set `L0=s+d-k`. This is positive for the inherited hierarchy: its exact mass formula is

\[
C_j=\frac{a}{c-2}c^j+\left(1-\frac{a}{c-2}\right)2^j,
\]

where `0<a/(c-2)<1`; thus `k_j/C_j<c-2<1/2` in the stated parameter range. Hence `L0>=C-k>0`.

Write `G_R=w-y=n_p-n_q-kp-dq`. Expansion of the two squares defining V_R and the two weighted sums gives

\[
V_R+\mathcal A_p+\mathcal A_q
=L_0(\langle n_p,p\rangle-\langle n_q,q\rangle)
-d I_p-k I_q+k^2\|p\|^2-(d+s)^2\|q\|^2.
\tag{4.4}
\]

Since `U_1(v)-U_d(y)=||p||^2-d||q||^2+2<n_p,p>-2<n_q,q>`, (4.4) is equivalent to

\[
\begin{aligned}
L_0[U_1(v)-U_d(y)]={}&2V_R+2\mathcal A_p+2\mathcal A_q
+2d I_p+2k I_q\\
&+(L_0-2k^2)\|p\|^2
+[2(d+s)^2-dL_0]\|q\|^2.
\tag{4.5}
\end{aligned}
\]

This is a retained actual-normal version of the sufficient-decrease calculation: the signed V_R is still visible. The source check of Zhang--Jiang's Definitions 4--7 and Section 3.1 shows that their primitive sufficient-decrease use requires the right child to be primitive. That hypothesis is not supplied for higher H_j here. The equations above are derived directly, without importing it. [Primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2410.12395v2).

The elementary parent square expansion is

\[
V_P=V_L+V_R+h\{U_1(v)-U_d(y)
+(s-k)\|p\|^2-(d+2s+h)\|q\|^2\}.
\]

Inserting (4.5) yields the full recurrence

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
V_P={}&V_L+\left(1+\frac{2h}{L_0}\right)V_R
+\frac{h A_0}{L_0}(\|p\|^2-\|q\|^2)\\
&+\frac{2h}{L_0}(\mathcal A_p+\mathcal A_q+d I_p+k I_q),\\
A_0={}&(C-k)(s+d)-kC-k^2.
\end{aligned}}
\tag{4.6}
\]

For `d=1`, `A0=C^2-2Ck-k^2`. This is the full signed identity, not just the scalar joining capacity. Its actual retained cross-subtree rows vanish on the interval equality trajectory in Section 3 because both `n_p` and `n_q` vanish. Thus these specific added rows cannot, by themselves, supply a uniform positive surplus on that mode.

The right-child multiplier in (4.6) is greater than one. At limiting silver parameters and d=1 it equals `c0^2`. A useful lower bound on the signed V_R must therefore be propagated quantitatively; replacing it by zero would assume unproved higher primitive positivity.

The negative root sign also occurs within the actual inherited depth range, not only in a formal fixed-epsilon extension. Write

\[
\frac{k_j}{C_j}=\frac{c-2}{1+\tau(2/c)^j},\qquad
\tau=\frac{c(c-2)-\beta}{\beta}
=\frac{(5+2\sqrt2)\epsilon+c_0^2\epsilon^2}{1-\epsilon}.
\]

For `epsilon<=1/6144`, `0<tau<9epsilon`. For every `j>=3`,

`a0 tau(2/c)^j < 72epsilon/c0^4 < c0 epsilon`,

where the last inequality uses `c0^5=41+29sqrt(2)>72`. Since `c-2=a0+c0 epsilon`, it follows that `k_j/C_j>a0`. Thus `A0=C^2-2Ck-k^2<0` at d=1 for every actual inherited `3<=j<m`. Its gradient-norm difference has no fixed sign on actual bodies. The signed right-child energy and this root term prevent concluding a nonnegative V or a sufficient discounted U law by dropping the remaining terms.

On the limiting interval trajectory both child energies are exactly zero, and (4.2) recovers precisely the discount surplus in (3.4)--(3.6). This checks the second transport against the actual obstruction. It does not justify imposing `V_L,V_R>=0` on other inherited data. No all-depth lower estimate for the signed-energy/normal combination in (4.6), and no replacement joint invariant that supplies one, was obtained within this grant.

## 5. Audit, scope, and stop condition

The principal draft result is the actual arbitrarily deep failure of the completed discarded-surplus payment, not a new unpaid matrix proposed as progress toward closure. Its proof checks the entire scalar physical path, both actual input projections, the parent endpoint transport, all relevant normal values, the negative left discount square, the signs at all trailing parameters in the general identity, and the transfer from limiting parameters to genuine same-enclosing-m children. The interval has an attained original minimizer zero; no radius change is used. The example is not invoked as a full-class worst-case upper or lower certificate.

The original U target is not refuted. The common two-remainder invariant is not refuted. In fact, Section 3 gives positive child and parent invariant surplus for the selected constants while the proposed payment expression is negative. Thus no conclusion about the truth of the target is drawn from failure of this warrant.

No final all-depth coefficient recurrence was established. There is no complete full-class asymptotic separation, no all-schedule lower proof, and no claim that the original minimax problem is solved. A finished-object independent adversarial audit was not triggered because there is no complete target-level candidate. The explicit identities and obstruction received manual text/source checks only; Danus verification has not accepted them.
