# Exact Constant Matching Lower Bound for Predetermined-Step Gradient Descent

Resolve the following minimax problem completely.

The objective is not merely to recover the silver exponent. The goal is to determine whether the best possible predetermined nonnegative stepsize schedule for gradient descent matches, asymptotically and with the correct leading constant/profile, the objective-value schedule constructed by Zehao Zhang and Rujun Jiang.

Throughout, “complexity” means the convergence/iteration-complexity rate as a function of the number of gradient steps \(n\), not the computational complexity of constructing a stepsize schedule.

---

## 1. Optimization model

Let \(L>0\), and let \(\mathcal C^{1,1}_L\) denote the class of all finite-dimensional convex differentiable functions

\[
f:\mathbb R^d\to\mathbb R
\]

whose gradient is \(L\)-Lipschitz and which possess at least one minimizer \(x_\star\).

For a predetermined nonnegative stepsize schedule

\[
h=(h_0,\ldots,h_{n-1})\in[0,\infty)^n,
\]

gradient descent is

\[
x_{t+1}
=
x_t-\frac{h_t}{L}\nabla f(x_t),
\qquad
t=0,\ldots,n-1.
\]

The schedule may depend on the horizon \(n\), but it must be chosen before seeing \(f\), \(x_0\), gradients, function values, or any other oracle information.

Define the normalized worst-case objective error

\[
\mathcal W_n(h)
:=
\sup
\frac{2\bigl(f(x_n)-f_\star\bigr)}
{L\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},
\]

where the supremum is over

- every finite dimension \(d\);
- every \(f\in\mathcal C^{1,1}_L\);
- every minimizer \(x_\star\in\arg\min f\);
- every \(x_0\neq x_\star\).

Define the minimax value

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n^\star
:=
\inf_{h\in[0,\infty)^n}\mathcal W_n(h).
}
\]

The normalization is chosen so that it agrees directly with the constants in Zhang–Jiang.

If instead one uses the normalization

\[
\mathcal R_n(h)
=
\sup
\frac{f(x_n)-f_\star}{L\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},
\]

then

\[
\mathcal W_n(h)=2\mathcal R_n(h).
\]

Do not mix the two normalizations.

---

## 2. The Zhang–Jiang benchmark

Write

\[
\rho
:=
\log_2(1+\sqrt2)
=
1.271553303\ldots.
\]

Define

\[
\varphi(x,y)
=
\frac{
-x-y+\sqrt{(x+y+2)^2+4(x+1)(y+1)}
}{2},
\]

and

\[
\psi(x,y)
=
\frac{
3-2y+\sqrt{(2y+1)(2y+8x+9)}
}{4}.
\]

Define two scalar sequences recursively.

First,

\[
S_\circ(0)=0,
\]

and for every \(n\ge1\),

\[
\boxed{
S_\circ(n)
=
\max_{0\le k<n}
\Bigl[
S_\circ(k)
+
\varphi\!\bigl(S_\circ(k),S_\circ(n-k-1)\bigr)
+
S_\circ(n-k-1)
\Bigr].
}
\]

Second,

\[
S_\bullet(0)=0,
\]

and for every \(n\ge1\),

\[
\boxed{
S_\bullet(n)
=
\max_{0\le k<n}
\Bigl[
S_\circ(k)
+
\psi\!\bigl(S_\circ(k),S_\bullet(n-k-1)\bigr)
+
S_\bullet(n-k-1)
\Bigr].
}
\]

These are the sums of the primitive and dominant schedules generated by the Zhang–Jiang concatenation dynamic program.

Let \(h_\bullet^{(n)}\) be any Zhang–Jiang dominant schedule attaining \(S_\bullet(n)\).

Their analysis gives the exact worst-case objective profile

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n\!\left(h_\bullet^{(n)}\right)
=
\frac{1}{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\]

The equality is important: the associated Huber instance attains the bound.

Define

\[
\boxed{
U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
:=
\frac{1}{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\]

Consequently,

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\le
U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
\qquad
\text{for every }n.
\]

This exact sequence, rather than the decimal \(0.4239\), is the benchmark that must be matched.

---

## 3. Exact problem to resolve

Determine whether

\[
\boxed{
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
=
1.
}
\tag{P}
\]

Equivalently, determine whether

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n^\star
\ge
\frac{1-o(1)}
{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\tag{LB}
\]

Since the reverse inequality

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\le
\frac{1}{2S_\bullet(n)+1}
\]

is already supplied by the Zhang–Jiang schedule, proving (LB) establishes asymptotic minimax optimality of their schedule horizon by horizon.

The quantifiers in (LB) are strict:

for every \(\varepsilon>0\), there must exist \(N_\varepsilon<\infty\) such that for every \(n\ge N_\varepsilon\) and every predetermined schedule \(h\in[0,\infty)^n\),

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n(h)
\ge
\frac{1-\varepsilon}
{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\tag{Q}
\]

The threshold \(N_\varepsilon\) must be independent of the particular schedule \(h\).

The adversarial function and its dimension may depend on \(n\) and on \(h\).

---

## 4. What “matching the complexity and constant” means

A complete affirmative solution must prove (P), not merely the silver exponent.

In particular, since

\[
S_\bullet(n)=\Theta(n^\rho),
\]

(P) automatically implies the optimal polynomial convergence exponent

\[
\rho=\log_2(1+\sqrt2).
\]

It also matches the complete asymptotic constant profile, including any genuine oscillation of \(S_\bullet(n)/n^\rho\).

For reference, define

\[
\bar\nu_\bullet
:=
\liminf_{n\to\infty}
\frac{S_\bullet(n)}{n^\rho}
\]

and

\[
C_{\mathrm{ZJ}}
:=
\frac{1}{2\bar\nu_\bullet}.
\]

Then

\[
\limsup_{n\to\infty}
n^\rho U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
=
C_{\mathrm{ZJ}}.
\]

Therefore (P) implies

\[
\boxed{
\limsup_{n\to\infty}
n^\rho\mathcal W_n^\star
=
C_{\mathrm{ZJ}}.
}
\]

However, proving only this limsup identity is weaker than (P) and does **not** count as a complete affirmative solution.

Likewise, proving merely

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star=\Theta(n^{-\rho})
\]

does not count.

---

## 5. Important warning about the number \(0.4239\)

Do not formulate the theorem as

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\sim
0.4239\,n^{-\rho}.
\]

That is not the precise Zhang–Jiang theorem.

Their rigorous asymptotic statement is expressed through

\[
\bar\nu_\bullet
=
\liminf_{n\to\infty}
S_\bullet(n)/n^\rho
\]

and the coefficient

\[
(2\bar\nu_\bullet)^{-1}.
\]

The frequently quoted number \(0.4239\) comes from an additional numerical estimate for \(\bar\nu_\bullet\), and is not itself the exact rigorously identified asymptotic constant.

The paper also notes numerical evidence for still smaller constants.

Thus:

- \(0.4239\) may be used as a numerical sanity check;
- it may not be assumed as an exact constant;
- a proof that only matches \(0.4239\) numerically is not sufficient;
- the rigorous benchmark is \(U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}=(2S_\bullet(n)+1)^{-1}\).

---

## 6. Current lower-bound benchmark

A recent lower bound of Ye and Liu considers, after setting \(L=1\),

\[
\mathcal R_n(h)
=
\sup_{f,x_0}
\frac{f(x_n)-f_\star}
{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},
\qquad
r_n^\star
=
\inf_{h\ge0}\mathcal R_n(h),
\]

and proves that for some absolute \(C>0\),

\[
r_n^\star
\ge
n^{-\left(
\rho+
C\sqrt{\frac{\log\log n}{\log n}}
\right)}
\]

for all sufficiently large \(n\).

Equivalently, in the normalization of this problem,

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\ge
2
n^{-\left(
\rho+
C\sqrt{\frac{\log\log n}{\log n}}
\right)}.
\]

This establishes the correct polynomial exponent only up to a subpolynomial loss.

It does **not** establish (P).

Removing the exponent loss alone is also not sufficient: the leading asymptotic constant/profile must be matched.

---

## 7. Existing hard-function tool

The Ye–Liu lower bound may be used as a starting point.

For checkpoints

\[
1\le t_1<\cdots<t_k\le n,
\]

write

\[
b_i=h_{t_i},
\qquad
s_i
=
\sum_{j=t_{i-1}+1}^{t_i-1}h_j,
\]

with \(t_0=0\), and let \(s_{k+1}\) denote the sum of the steps after the last checkpoint.

For suitable parameters \(\epsilon,\gamma>0\), putting

\[
c=\sqrt{1+2\gamma+\epsilon^2},
\]

their construction yields constants \(a=a(\epsilon,\gamma)\) and \(B_0=B_0(\epsilon)\) such that, when every checkpoint obeys \(b_i\ge B_0\),

\[
\mathcal R_n(h)
\ge
\frac{1}{4(1+s_{k+1})}
\prod_{i=1}^k
\left[
\frac{b_i}
{c(a+s_i)+\epsilon b_i}
\right]^2.
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n(h)
\ge
\frac{1}{2(1+s_{k+1})}
\prod_{i=1}^k
\left[
\frac{b_i}
{c(a+s_i)+\epsilon b_i}
\right]^2.
}
\tag{Y--L}
\]

You may sharpen, replace, or completely abandon this construction.

Do not assume that merely optimizing its existing parameters is sufficient.

A central obstruction in the current proof is that the auxiliary constant \(a(\epsilon,\gamma)\) deteriorates rapidly when parameters are sent toward the regime needed to recover the exact silver exponent. Any proposed constant-level proof must track such losses quantitatively and cannot hide them inside \(o(1)\) unless that \(o(1)\) is genuinely negligible relative to \(U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}\).

---

## 8. Acceptable complete resolutions

Assume for purposes of this task that the question admits a rigorous resolution, but do not assume in advance that the answer to (P) is affirmative.

### Affirmative resolution

Prove

\[
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
=
1.
\]

Because the upper bound is known, the substantive requirement is the uniform lower bound (Q).

A complete proof must apply to every sufficiently large horizon \(n\) and every predetermined nonnegative schedule of length \(n\).

### Negative resolution

Prove rigorously that (P) is false.

For example, it is sufficient to establish that there exist

\[
\delta>0,
\qquad
n_j\to\infty,
\qquad
h^{(j)}\in[0,\infty)^{n_j},
\]

such that

\[
\mathcal W_{n_j}\!\left(h^{(j)}\right)
\le
(1-\delta)
U_{n_j}^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
\]

for every \(j\), with a rigorous worst-case upper-bound certificate.

Equivalently, it is sufficient to prove

\[
\liminf_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
<1.
\]

Numerical evidence is not a negative resolution.

Failure to prove the lower bound is not evidence that it is false.

---

## 9. Results that do not count as a solution

Do **not** stop for any of the following:

- reproducing the known \(n^{-\rho-o(1)}\) lower bound;
- proving \(n^{-\rho-\varepsilon}\) for every fixed \(\varepsilon>0\);
- proving only that \(\rho\) is the optimal polynomial exponent;
- proving \(\mathcal W_n^\star=\Theta(n^{-\rho})\);
- obtaining an unspecified positive constant times \(n^{-\rho}\);
- obtaining a constant strictly smaller than the Zhang–Jiang benchmark;
- proving the desired constant only along \(n=2^m-1\);
- proving it along any other subsequence;
- proving only a limsup or liminf lower bound;
- proving only
  \[
  \limsup n^\rho\mathcal W_n^\star=C_{\mathrm{ZJ}};
  \]
- showing that a candidate hard instance works for the silver schedule but not for every schedule;
- restricting stepsizes to a bounded interval;
- assuming stepsizes are monotone, ordered, separated, or bounded away from zero;
- assuming the schedule has a prescribed recursive/composable form;
- assuming all large steps can be selected as checkpoints without proving this;
- proving a lower bound only for Zhang–Jiang's class of concatenated schedules;
- proving a lower bound only for “basic”, “composable”, periodic, symmetric, or silver-like schedules;
- replacing nonnegative schedules by positive schedules without treating zero steps;
- permitting the adversary to choose the schedule after seeing the function;
- allowing adaptive stepsizes, momentum, averaging, restarting, or additional state;
- proving a result for gradient norm rather than last-iterate objective gap;
- solving the anytime version instead of the finite-horizon non-anytime problem;
- changing the performance normalization;
- dropping the factor \(1/2\) in the Zhang–Jiang normalization;
- treating \(0.4239\) as an exact proven constant;
- using floating-point calculations as proof of an asymptotic constant;
- producing a stronger lower bound than the Zhang–Jiang upper bound without noticing the resulting contradiction;
- hiding a subpolynomial loss inside a claimed constant;
- obtaining a bound of the form
  \[
  n^{-\rho}\exp[-o(\log n)]
  \]
  rather than
  \[
  (1-o(1))U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}};
  \]
- proving finite-\(n\) inequalities for finitely many computed horizons;
- providing only a conjectured extremal schedule;
- providing only a conjectured hard function;
- reducing the problem to an unproved statement of essentially the same minimax strength;
- reporting a promising lemma, numerical pattern, or “substantial progress”.

Intermediate positive progress is not a stopping condition.

---

## 10. Proof standards

Every invoked theorem must be stated with the hypotheses needed here.

In particular, carefully verify:

- convexity;
- \(L\)-smoothness;
- existence of a minimizer;
- the exact GD recursion;
- nonnegativity of all stepsizes;
- the distinction between a schedule chosen for a fixed horizon and an infinite anytime schedule;
- the normalization by \(L\|x_0-x_\star\|^2/2\);
- uniformity over every schedule \(h\in[0,\infty)^n\);
- whether constants are absolute or parameter dependent;
- every use of \(o(1)\), including its sign and uniformity;
- every limit interchange;
- every passage between \(\mathcal R_n\) and \(\mathcal W_n\);
- every use of finite-dimensional interpolation;
- every claim that a constructed collection of values and gradients is realizable by a convex \(L\)-smooth function.

A lower-bound construction may depend on \(n\) and on the entire predetermined schedule \(h\), because the worst-case function is chosen after the schedule.

Its dimension may grow with \(n\).

---

## 11. Promising proof families to investigate

Use genuinely different approaches rather than repeatedly varying one incomplete argument.

Relevant families include:

- exact smooth-convex interpolation and primal PEP constructions;
- dual PEP certificates and complementary slackness;
- deriving a dual dynamic program to the Zhang–Jiang concatenation recursion;
- minimax/Bellman formulations in which \(\varphi\) and \(\psi\) arise from primal-dual optimality;
- hard functions whose recursive composition exactly mirrors ConPP/ConPD;
- sharper checkpoint constructions without the \(\epsilon\)-dependent constant explosion;
- curved-gradient or Moreau-envelope constructions with exact rather than approximate transfer factors;
- piecewise quadratic or Huber-type interpolation constructions;
- finite-dimensional resisting-oracle constructions;
- composition/duality of extremal first-order methods;
- exact characterization of the PEP value for arbitrary nonnegative stepsizes;
- identification of a scalar functional \(V(h)\) satisfying both a universal lower bound and the same concatenation recursion as \(S_\bullet\);
- structural reductions proving that an arbitrary schedule can be decomposed at essentially no constant loss;
- induction on \(n\) with a sharp split inequality;
- continuous scaling limits of the recursion, but only when converted back to rigorous discrete estimates with the correct constant;
- exact algebraic or semialgebraic certificates for small horizons that reveal the correct general recursion.

Do not assume one of these approaches must succeed.

---

## 12. Multiagent search protocol

Use multiagent v2 aggressively and dynamically, with up to four concurrent agents if available.

Begin with a genuinely diverse portfolio.

Do not tell most early agents what the currently favored proof route is.

Maintain an explicit registry of mathematical approach families, organized by actual mechanism rather than wording.

If multiple agents converge to the same checkpoint construction, PEP dual, or recurrence argument, redirect some agents to genuinely different formulations.

Keep both possibilities alive:

1. the Zhang–Jiang schedule is asymptotically minimax optimal;
2. another schedule beats its constant/profile.

Do not discard the second possibility merely because the exponents match.

When an approach reaches a missing lemma whose strength is essentially equivalent to (Q), mark the route as blocked. Do not call that lemma “technical” or “routine”.

Only reopen such a route if a new mechanism appears.

---

## 13. Computational agents

Use computation as an adversarial and discovery tool, not as proof.

For moderate \(n\), agents should attempt to compute or tightly bound:

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star,
\qquad
U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}},
\qquad
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}},
\]

using exact or high-precision PEP/SDP/branch-and-bound methods where possible.

They should also:

- compute \(S_\circ(n)\) and \(S_\bullet(n)\);
- inspect the maximizing split indices;
- search for schedules beating \(h_\bullet^{(n)}\);
- test proposed universal hard-function inequalities;
- search for counterexamples to intermediate lemmas;
- inspect primal and dual PEP optimizers;
- identify rank patterns and active interpolation constraints;
- attempt rational/algebraic reconstruction of numerical certificates;
- verify normalization factors independently.

A numerical counterexample to a proposed lemma is useful and should kill that lemma.

A numerical confirmation of the main conjecture is not a proof.

---

## 14. Adversarial audit

Every proposed complete affirmative proof must be independently attacked for at least the following failure modes:

- it actually proves only the exponent;
- a fixed \(\varepsilon>0\) remains in the exponent;
- \(\varepsilon=\varepsilon_n\to0\) creates a hidden constant blowup;
- it proves only infinitely many horizons;
- it proves only dyadic horizons;
- its \(o(1)\) depends on the schedule;
- its hard function is not globally convex and smooth;
- the interpolation data are inconsistent;
- the minimizer used in the normalization is not valid;
- the dimension is implicitly infinite without justification;
- very large stepsizes are excluded;
- zero stepsizes break the argument;
- a checkpoint-selection lemma silently loses a constant;
- a product inequality loses a nonvanishing factor;
- a factor \(2\) is lost when translating between normalizations;
- the Zhang–Jiang constant is replaced by \(0.4239\);
- a limsup statement is mistaken for horizon-by-horizon asymptotics;
- a numerical observation is used inside a supposedly analytic proof;
- the proof establishes optimality only within a restricted schedule family;
- a PEP dual certificate is feasible only numerically rather than exactly;
- strong duality is invoked without verifying its hypotheses;
- an asymptotic recurrence is substituted for the exact discrete one;
- the final lower bound exceeds a known rigorous upper bound because of a normalization error.

A proof survives only if all such issues are resolved explicitly.

---

## 15. Output requirements

Do not return an exploratory essay.

Do not return a research diary.

Do not return a collection of partial lemmas.

Do not return a “most promising approach”.

Do not return merely because several approaches fail.

Do not return merely because agents agree that the problem is difficult.

Do not return a positive intermediate result.

Return only one of the following:

### A. Complete affirmative proof

A rigorous proof of

\[
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
=
1,
\]

including every necessary lemma and every constant/normalization check.

### B. Complete negative proof

A rigorous proof that the above limit is not \(1\), with an explicit asymptotic separation or another logically conclusive disproof.

Before returning either answer, subject the argument to an independent adversarial audit and repair every identified gap.

If the argument still contains an unproved lemma of theorem-level strength, the problem is not resolved and you must continue working.

Do not label a missing minimax inequality, uniformity statement, interpolation theorem, structural decomposition, or constant-preserving recursion as “routine”.

The stopping criterion is mathematical completion, not positive progress.

---

## 16. Literature usage

The following sources may be treated as background starting points, but their statements and normalizations must be checked carefully:

- Zehao Zhang and Rujun Jiang, *Accelerated Gradient Descent by Concatenation of Stepsize Schedules*, arXiv:2410.12395 / SIAM Journal on Optimization.
- Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, *Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152 and the accompanying lower-bound exposition.
- standard smooth-convex interpolation and PEP results;
- standard results concerning the silver stepsize schedule and concatenation constructions.

Public search may be used for standard named theorems, definitions, and the cited papers.

Do not use public search merely to locate a posted solution to this exact benchmark.

The task is to produce and verify the mathematical resolution itself.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001514 -->
# Danus — main-agent operating contract (codex)

You are the main agent of Danus, an automated mathematics proof-search system: the
operator's entry point and orchestrator. Workers (codex) prove, the verify service
is the sole correctness authority, and you steer — you do not do the math yourself.
Full contract: `agents/contracts/main_agent.md`. The architecture map and module
index is `ARCHITECTURE.md`.

**Read `OPERATOR.md` now** — it is your durable operator profile (how to address
them, language, spend ceiling, consult transport, standing preferences). It is not
auto-loaded; read it at the start of every session and follow it.

## Start here — initialize before anything else on a new session

The first thing you do, before summarizing the repo or answering "what should we
do", is check whether this deployment is initialized. It is not initialized if
`runtime/.danus-initialized` is absent (corroborating signs: still on `main`, no
`config/danus.env`, or `OPERATOR.md` is still the blank template).

- **If not initialized:** do not start work and do not just describe the repo.
  Greet the operator, explain Danus in 2–3 sentences, and invoke the `initialize`
  skill. It interviews them (operating choices, how to address them, git branch,
  spend ceiling, consult transport `gpt_pro`/`claude_api`/`claude_code`/`off`, codex backend),
  provisions `OPERATOR.md` and `config/danus.env`, starts the verify service, and
  marks `runtime/.danus-initialized`. Setup is not optional.
- **If already initialized:** re-read `OPERATOR.md` and the relevant
  project's `PROBLEM.md`, then help.

## Working style

- Reply to the operator in their language per `OPERATOR.md` (code, comments, skills,
  and commits stay English).
- Honesty — never fake success. State only what you verified (checked exit status,
  re-read the file, saw the fact land). On an error, a forbidden action, or an empty
  result, report and quote it; no silent retry-and-claim-done. If unsure, say so.

## Environment

You run rooted at this repo dir — that is why this `AGENTS.md`, `.codex/config.toml`,
and `.agents/skills/` load. For anything visual, bind to `127.0.0.1` and hand the
operator the port-forward + URL (e.g. dashboard on `:8099`). Secrets live only in
gitignored `config/*.env` (never elsewhere). The codex backend is BYO key
(`config/codex.env`); confirm with `bash scripts/check-codex.sh`.

## Persist what you ask — you forget at session end

Only what you write to disk survives. Persist every operator-given fact / fork
decision to its home immediately:

| info | durable home |
| --- | --- |
| operator profile & standing prefs | `OPERATOR.md` (read it at session start) |
| a project's problem / goal (verbatim) | `runtime/projects/<p>/PROBLEM.md` |
| the finalized target theorem (write-paper reads this) | `runtime/projects/<p>/TARGET.md` — the default paper; a non-default paper → `papers/<paper_id>/TARGET.md` (via `danus finalize [--paper <id>]`) |
| evolving strategy | global memory `master_guidance` / `elaboration` (`gm_add`) |
| secrets (tokens, API keys) | `config/*.env` (gitignored) — never anywhere else |

## Orchestrate

A project is the unit of work: its own problem, workers, `global_memory/`, and
`fact_graph/`, isolated under `runtime/projects/<p>/`. Run several at once; every
memory or fact op names a project (there is no default).

**Control surface** — danus MCP (role=main): `gm_add` · `gm_search` · `fact_search`
· `fact_revoke` · `search_arxiv_theorems` (first four take `project=`; you have no
`fact_submit`, so you never write facts). `danus` CLI: `list`/`new`/`assign`/
`finalize`/`start`/`status`/`stop` (see `danus/orchestration`). Skills (`.agents/skills/`): `elaboration` ·
`consult` · `human-summary` · `write-paper`. Dashboard: `scripts/services.sh up
dashboard <p>` + port-forward.

**Strategic loop** (per project, on genuine new state only): elaborate
(`elaboration` skill → `gm_add`) → consult a top-tier model (`consult` over the
`gpt_pro`, `claude_api`, or `claude_code` transport; `off` = you reason on your own) → record the reply as
`master_guidance` + `danus assign` each worker → monitor. At project start, ask the
worker roster (how many `high` + `xhigh`; default `high:3,xhigh:4`), write
`PROBLEM.md`, then `danus new <project> --roles high:N,xhigh:M`.

## Operating mode (single, attended)

While your session is active you are the main agent: periodic summary (~1h), consult
(~2h), coordination, and live plan adjustment — pace your own monitoring beats.
While inactive, only the workers keep looping. Run only one main agent at a time.

**Completion:** the moment every target of a project is a verified fact **and** the
route is credible, `danus stop <project>` the swarm yourself (graceful) — act, then
notify; do not wait for the operator. This is the one time you wind a project down
on your own; a slow or hard problem never is. Declaring the result as *the answer*
(`danus finalize`) stays a fork you surface.

## Persistent services — the system does not run without them

```bash
bash scripts/services.sh up verify          # REQUIRED — no verify ⇒ fact_submit fails ⇒ no facts
bash scripts/services.sh up dashboard <p>   # optional view (then port-forward)
bash scripts/services.sh status | logs <svc> [-f] | down <svc>|all
```

Start them only via `services.sh` (it `setsid`-detaches each so it survives your
session ending); a bare `&` dies with your session. Ensure `verify` is up before
starting any workers. On a flaky codex backend, `bash scripts/check-codex.sh`.

## Never cross these layers

- No math yourself; no reading worker local memory — read shared state via
  `gm_search` / `fact_search`.
- No hand-editing the truth stores — only `gm_add` / `fact_revoke` / the `danus`
  commands. The fact graph is the one source of truth (verifier-accepted,
  content-addressed); a fact enters only via a worker's `fact_submit`; you never
  fabricate one (you structurally cannot).

## Surface these forks to the operator (then persist the decision)

Finalizing a verified result as *the answer* · `fact_revoke` (cascades) · anything
outward (a `git push`, arXiv, a LaTeX-git push — confirm anything that leaves the
machine) · paid-API consult spend past the operator's ceiling · the codex backend
persistently failing · anything you are genuinely unsure about. Everything else:
act, then log and notify.

## Git

Branch off `main` at init (`git checkout -b deploy/<operator>`). Commit each requested change with a clear message. Never `git push`
automatically — only when the operator asks. Never commit `config/*.env` or
`runtime/` (both gitignored).

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001515 -->
---
fact_id: 1252b8823bb5a290
problem_id: gd-exact-profile-20260926
author: max2
predecessors: []
glossary_introduces:
  A_quad: Diagonal positive quadratic Hessian with distinct eigenvalues strictly between0 and1, used for strict feasibility.
  D_i: Elementary normalized trajectory bound product_(t<i)(1+h_t).
  E_red: All distinct ordered prefix pairs together with all outgoing terminal pairs (n,j), j in J_pre.
  G_pr: Full primal Gram matrix of the formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), or its fixed point in the separation argument.
  H_j: Conjugate lower quadratic cjet_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||²/2 for a prefix jet.
  J_pre: Prefix sample index set {star,0,...,n-1}, or {star} for n=0.
  M_cert: Full formal-vector quadratic certificate q_cert||x_0||²-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij after GD substitution.
  Mhat_cert: Full PSD certificate matrix for the positive compressed schedule.
  Q_ij: Signed normalized smooth-convex interpolation residual f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||²/2.
  R_alpha: The complete prefix quadratic remainder in M_cert=||g_n-V_alpha||²+R_alpha.
  T_pick: Linear coordinate-selection map from all original formal vectors to those used by the compressed certificate.
  V_alpha: x_n-sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j y_j, independent of the formal terminal gradient.
  alpha_j: Nonnegative terminal outgoing probability lambda_(n,j)/2 for j in J_pre.
  cjet_j: Conjugate jet value <g_j,x_j>-f_j in this terminal-extension result.
  delta_j: Prefix value f_j-||g_j||²/2, with delta_star=0.
  ghat_s: Compressed formal gradient chosen from the original positive-step indices, with ghat_m=g_n.
  hhat: Positive-step compression of the original nonnegative schedule.
  i_s: Original index of the s-th positive step in the zero-step compression argument.
  iota: Injection of compressed sample indices into the original indices, mapping the compressed terminal to original n.
  lambda^pre: Nonnegative interpolation multipliers on the complete queried prefix.
  lambda_ij: Nonnegative multiplier for the signed ordered interpolation residual Q_ij.
  lambdahat: Tight full certificate for the positive compressed schedule.
  q_cert: A real objective upper-certificate level; selected tight level is W_n(h).
  v(x): Unique vector maximizing <x,v>-H(v) in the explicitly constructed conjugate F.
  w_opt: The exact reduced/full objective value W_n(h) used in the separating-hyperplane argument.
  w_t: Cumulative terminal probability alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j, with w_(-1)=alpha_star.
  xhat_s: Compressed formal position x_0-sum_(r<s)h_(i_r)g_(i_r), equal to the mapped original position.
  y_j: Prefix proximal point x_j-g_j, with y_star=0.
external_refs: []
---

## statement
Exact unqueried-terminal reduction and full-certificate re-selection for arbitrary nonnegative gradient-descent schedules.

For every integer n>=0, every L>0, and every finite h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) in [0,infinity)^n, let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, with x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). The empty schedule n=0 is allowed.

For the following finite formulations use normalized smoothness1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0, normalized sampled values f_i, formal gradients g_i, and x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. Put I_n={star,0,...,n}, J_pre={star,0,...,n-1}, and Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 for distinct i,j in I_n. When n=0, J_pre={star}.

(a) The finite Gram maximization of 2f_n subject to G_pr>=0, G_pr_(x0,x0)<=1, all Q_ij<=0 with distinct i,j in J_pre, and all Q_(n,j)<=0 with j in J_pre, has attained optimal value W_n(h). Here G_pr is the full Gram matrix of the n+2 formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), with no rank constraint. No incoming terminal inequality Q_(j,n)<=0 is imposed. For any feasible prefix jets in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space, define cjet_j=<g_j,x_j>-f_j, H_j(v)=cjet_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||^2/2, H(v)=max_(j in J_pre)H_j(v), and F(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H(v)). Then F is an everywhere finite convex differentiable 1-smooth function interpolating all prefix jets, F(0)=0=min F, and it is the pointwise maximal such prefix interpolant. For every reduced-feasible terminal pair (g_n,f_n), F(x_n)>=f_n. At an optimal reduced transcript, F(x_n)=f_n and grad F(x_n)=g_n; hence every optimal reduced transcript is fully interpolable and obeys the deleted incoming terminal inequalities.

(b) There exist nonnegative multipliers lambda_ij on all distinct pairs in I_n, with lambda_(j,n)=0 for every j in J_pre, finite-node flow sum_j lambda_ij-sum_j lambda_ji=2*1_(i=n), and a FULL positive semidefinite quadratic certificate
M_cert=W_n(h)||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij
after the GD substitution. This certificate is PSD on the original formal space (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), including every gradient at a zero step, without imposing equality between duplicated formal gradients. Thus some tight full certificate has no incoming edge at the unqueried terminal sample for every nonnegative schedule. For schedules with positive steps this follows from an explicit strict primal and a separation argument. If there are zero steps, choose the original index of each positive step as the representative of its compressed query and the original index n as the terminal representative; embedding a certificate for the positive-step compression gives the asserted full-space certificate. The all-zero and empty schedules have W_n(h)=1 and the direct certificate lambda_(n,star)=2, all other lambda zero, with M_cert=||x_0-g_n||^2.

(c) For any real certificate level q_cert and any h as above, certificates with no incoming terminal multipliers are parameterized exactly as follows. Choose alpha_j>=0 for j in J_pre, with sum_j alpha_j=1, and nonnegative prefix multipliers lambda^pre_ij for distinct i,j in J_pre whose finite prefix flow is out_i^pre-in_i^pre=2alpha_i for 0<=i<n, where out_i^pre=sum_(j in J_pre,j!=i)lambda^pre_ij and in_i^pre=sum_(j in J_pre,j!=i)lambda^pre_ji. Define y_j=x_j-g_j, delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2, V_alpha=x_n-sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j y_j, with y_star=delta_star=0, and
R_alpha=q_cert||x_0||^2-2sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j delta_j-||V_alpha||^2+sum_(i!=j in J_pre)lambda^pre_ij Q_ij.
The prefix flow cancels all f_i in R_alpha. Set lambda_(n,j)=2alpha_j, all incoming terminal multipliers zero, and retain lambda^pre. The complete identity is
M_cert=||g_n-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha.
Consequently full PSD of M_cert is equivalent to PSD of the entire prefix quadratic R_alpha in (x_0,g_0,...,g_(n-1)). For n>=1, with w_(-1)=alpha_star and w_t=alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j, one has 0<=w_(-1)<=w_0<=...<=w_(n-1)=1 and
V_alpha=alpha_star x_0-sum_(t<n)[h_t w_t-(w_t-w_(t-1))]g_t.
For n=0, alpha_star=1, V_alpha=x_0, and R_alpha=(q_cert-1)||x_0||^2. Any actual normalized trajectory attaining a feasible q_cert has g_n=V_alpha, R_alpha=0, and multiplier complementarity.

This result deletes no interpolation inequalities inside the queried prefix. It asserts existence of a selected optimal certificate, not that every optimal certificate has the selected support. It neither establishes Huber tightness nor removes earlier remote forward edges, transfers earlier-cut stationarity under arbitrary re-selection, or proves the project's asymptotic exact-profile conjecture.

## proof
1. Normalization, interpolation necessity, and elementary bounds.

For an original instance let R=||x_0-x_star||>0. The normalized function
F_0(z)=[f(x_star+R z)-f(x_star)]/(L R^2)
is convex differentiable with gradient Lipschitz constant1, a minimizer0,
and initial norm1. Its GD uses the same h and its normalized objective
is 2F_0(z_n). This also proves the converse scaling correspondence.
We may work with smoothness1 and the optimum sample zero.

For a differentiable function K with 1-Lipschitz gradient, integration
of its gradient along a segment gives
K(x+d)<=K(x)+<grad K(x),d>+||d||^2/2.
For convex K, apply this to K(z)-<grad K(x_i),z>, whose minimum is
at x_i, taking a unit negative-gradient step from x_j. Its minimum
value is no larger than the stepped value, so
K(x_j)-K(x_i)-<grad K(x_i),x_j-x_i>
    >=||grad K(x_j)-grad K(x_i)||^2/2.
This is exactly Q_ij<=0 for the actual samples, with our stated
orientation of Q.

For a normalized legal instance, put D_i=product_(t<i)(1+h_t), D_0=1.
Since grad K(0)=0, ||g_i||<=||x_i|| and ||x_(i+1)||<=(1+h_i)||x_i||.
Thus ||x_i||,||g_i||<=D_i, and
0<=K(x_i)<=||x_i||^2/2.
In particular W_n(h)<=D_n^2<infinity.
It is strictly positive: choose the scalar quadratic K(x)=kappa*x^2/2
with kappa=1/[2(1+sum_t h_t)] in (0,1], starting at1.
Every factor 1-kappa h_t is positive, and its normalized error is
kappa*product_t(1-kappa h_t)^2>0.

2. Explicit interpolation and the maximal prefix extension.

Let any finite collection of prefix jets (x_j,g_j,f_j), j in J_pre,
satisfy every ordered prefix inequality and include the zero optimum jet.
Define cjet_j,H_j,H,F as in the statement, on the same finite-dimensional
Euclidean space. The function H(v)-||v||^2/2 is the maximum of finitely
many affine functions and hence is convex. H is continuous, finite,
1-strongly convex, and has quadratic growth. For each x, the function
<v,x>-H(v) therefore has a unique maximizer, denoted v(x).

The maximizer condition is x in partial H(v(x)). For any x,x',
the two strong subgradient inequalities for H, added together, give
<x-x',v(x)-v(x')>>=||v(x)-v(x')||^2.
Consequently ||v(x)-v(x')||<=||x-x'||.
The definition of F, evaluated at v(x) and v(x'), gives
0<=F(x')-F(x)-<v(x),x'-x>
    <=<v(x')-v(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.
Thus F is differentiable with grad F(x)=v(x), and its gradient is
1-Lipschitz. It is convex as a supremum of affine functions and finite
by the stated quadratic growth.

For i,j in J_pre direct expansion gives H_j(g_i)-a_i=Q_ij.
The term with j=i equals zero and every other term is nonpositive;
hence H(g_i)=a_i. The active quadratic H_i has gradient x_i at g_i,
and H>=H_i with equality there, so x_i is a subgradient of H at g_i.
The unique maximizer defining F(x_i) is consequently g_i, and
F(x_i)=<x_i,g_i>-a_i=f_i. This proves interpolation of all prefix jets.
In particular F(0)=0 and grad F(0)=0, so zero is a global minimizer.
The same construction, for any finite index set including the zero
jet, proves sufficiency of ALL pairwise Q inequalities for a legal
convex 1-smooth interpolant; repeated positions cause no exception.

If K is any other legal function interpolating the prefix, apply the
necessity calculation in paragraph1 between its sample at an arbitrary
x and each prefix jet j. Writing g=grad K(x), those inequalities give
H_j(g)<=<x,g>-K(x) for every j. Thus
K(x)<=<x,g>-H(g)<=F(x).
This proves the claimed pointwise maximality without importing an
interpolation or conjugacy theorem from a paper.

3. Exact value of the reduced Gram problem and primal attainment.

Realize any feasible Gram matrix by vectors in dimension at most n+2;
if its rank is zero, one may embed its zero vectors in R.
The complete prefix inequalities give the function F of paragraph2.
Its gradients at every queried prefix point equal the supplied g_i,
so the GD recursion reaches the same supplied x_n. No terminal gradient
is used to determine x_n, even when some steps vanish.

For the reduced terminal pair, the retained inequalities give
H_j(g_n)<=<x_n,g_n>-f_n for all j in J_pre. Therefore
f_n<=<x_n,g_n>-H(g_n)<=F(x_n).
If ||x_0||>0, normalization of this actual function yields
2F(x_n)<=W_n(h)||x_0||^2<=W_n(h).
If ||x_0||=0, all actual prefix iterates and x_n are the minimizer0,
so F(x_n)=0 and 2f_n<=0<W_n(h).
Thus the reduced supremum is at most W_n(h).
Every actual legal normalized trajectory is feasible for the reduced
Gram problem, so the reverse inequality holds.

For clarity the supremum is attained. Consider first the FULL Gram
problem, including every incoming terminal inequality. Its feasible
set is closed. Paragraph2 applied to the entire finite index set
I_n realizes every feasible point as a legal function; hence paragraph1
bounds all sampled gradients, all Gram entries by Cauchy--Schwarz,
and all values 0<=f_i<=D_i^2/2. The feasible set is therefore compact
in finite-dimensional matrix/value space. It is nonempty and has an
objective maximizer. Necessity and the interpolation construction
show its value is exactly W_n(h). The same full optimizer is reduced
feasible, so it also attains the reduced value.

At any reduced optimizer, the chain
W_n(h)=2f_n<=2F(x_n)<=W_n(h)||x_0||^2<=W_n(h)
has equal endpoints and W_n(h)>0. Thus ||x_0||=1,
f_n=F(x_n), and <x_n,g_n>-H(g_n)=F(x_n).
The maximizer of that conjugate expression is unique, so
g_n=grad F(x_n). The supplied terminal jet therefore belongs to the
same legal F as the prefix and satisfies every deleted incoming
inequality. This also proves the assertion for every reduced optimizer,
not just one optimizer chosen from the full feasible set.

4. Dual attainment for positive schedules, by separation.

Assume n>=1 and h_t>0 for every t<n. Let E_red be the retained
ordered pair set:
{(i,j): i,j in J_pre, i!=j} union {(n,j):j in J_pre}.
Use the matrix/value variables of the reduced primal.

There is a strictly feasible point with G_pr positive definite,
G_pr_(x0,x0)<1, and every retained Q strictly negative.
Choose n+2 distinct real theta_l in (0,1), let A_quad be the diagonal
matrix with these entries, let K(x)=<x,A_quad x>/2, and choose x_0
with every coordinate nonzero and norm less than1.
Write Poly_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z), including Poly_0=1.
The columns (x_0,g_0,...,g_n) are evaluations at the theta_l of the
n+2 polynomials 1,z Poly_0,...,z Poly_n, times nonzero initial
coordinate factors. These polynomials have distinct degrees
0,1,...,n+1 and nonzero leading coefficients. A polynomial of degree
at most n+1 vanishing at n+2 distinct nodes is zero, so the evaluation
matrix is invertible and G_pr is positive definite.
For i!=j finite, Poly_i-Poly_j is a nonzero polynomial of degree
at most n; it cannot vanish at all chosen nodes. Poly_i cannot vanish
at all nodes either. Thus all finite sample positions are distinct
from each other and from star. Direct substitution for this quadratic
gives, with I the identity matrix,
Q_ij=-<x_i-x_j,A_quad(I-A_quad)(x_i-x_j)>/2<0.
This proves the full stated strictness.

Let w_opt=W_n(h), which is finite by paragraph1. Consider the open
convex set O of tuples (s,t,c), with s in R^(E_red) and t,c real,
for which there exist G_pr>=0 and real
sample values f with
s_ij>Q_ij(f,G_pr) for ij in E_red,
t>G_pr_(x0,x0)-1, and c<2f_n.
It is open since it is a union of open orthants; it is convex by
linearity of every Q in the Gram/value variables and convexity
of the PSD cone. The point (0,0,w_opt) is not in O.
Finite-dimensional separation of a point from a nonempty open convex
set gives a nonzero linear functional, chosen nonnegative on O
relative to that point. Independent upward changes of each s_ij
and of t, and downward changes of c, force its coefficients to be
(lambda_ij,rho,-eta) with lambda_ij,rho,eta>=0.
Taking limits to the boundary of each strict inequality yields,
for ALL G_pr>=0 and ALL real sample values f,
sum_(E_red)lambda_ij Q_ij(f,G_pr)
    +rho(G_pr_(x0,x0)-1)-eta*2f_n >= -eta*w_opt.       (S)

If eta=0, some lambda_ij or rho is positive, since the functional is
nonzero. Substitution of the strict primal point constructed above
makes the left side of (S) strictly negative, a contradiction.
Hence eta>0 and we divide the coefficients by eta to set eta=1.

The f_i are unrestricted real variables in (S). Their coefficients
must vanish:
out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n), for 0<=i<=n.
After their cancellation, (S) says
trace(M_cert(rho,lambda,h) G_pr)-rho>=-w_opt for every G_pr>=0.
If this matrix had a negative quadratic direction, scaling the
corresponding rank-one PSD G_pr would contradict this inequality.
It is therefore PSD. Taking G_pr=0 gives rho<=w_opt.
Conversely the flow, lambda>=0, and full PSD give, for every reduced
feasible point,
2f_n<=rho G_pr_(x0,x0)<=rho
because rho>=0. Thus w_opt<=rho. Equality follows.
Setting lambda_(j,n)=0 on the omitted pairs gives the required
tight full certificate on the original positive-step formal space.

5. Zero-step compression and lifting in the ORIGINAL formal space.

Suppose n>=1 and exactly m>=1 of the original steps are positive.
List their original indices as
0<=i_0<i_1<...<i_(m-1)<=n-1,
and define the positive compressed schedule hhat_s=h_(i_s),
0<=s<m. Deleting zero steps changes no actual iterate; therefore
W_m(hhat)=W_n(h) by the same function/initial-point trajectories in
both directions.

Apply paragraph4 to hhat, obtaining a tight incoming-terminal-free
certificate lambdahat on {star,0,...,m}.
Define the injective index map
iota(star)=star, iota(s)=i_s for 0<=s<m, and iota(m)=n.
On the original formal space define
xhat_0=x_0, ghat_s=g_(i_s) for s<m, ghat_m=g_n.
Its compressed formal positions satisfy
xhat_s=x_0-sum_(r<s)h_(i_r)g_(i_r)=x_(i_s) for s<m,
and
xhat_m=x_0-sum_(r<m)h_(i_r)g_(i_r)=x_n.
These are polynomial identities in independent original formal vectors:
the omitted coefficients h_t are ZERO. They do not use any equality
between gradients sampled at identical actual positions.

Put lambda_(iota(s),iota(t))=lambdahat_(s,t) on mapped pairs, and
put every other original multiplier equal to zero.
At each mapped finite node the original flow equals its compressed
flow; at each unmapped node both in-flow and out-flow vanish.
The mapped terminal is n, so the original finite flow is exactly
out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n). No multiplier points into original n.
Every mapped Q expression equals the corresponding compressed
Q expression after identifying the mapped values, whose coefficients
cancel by flow.

Let T_pick be the linear map selecting
(x_0,g_(i_0),...,g_(i_(m-1)),g_n)
from (x_0,g_0,...,g_n). The ORIGINAL full certificate matrix is precisely
T_pick^T Mhat_cert T_pick and is PSD because Mhat_cert is PSD.
Unmapped gradient columns are zero. No duplicate-gradient constraint
or auxiliary squared penalty is imposed or needed. This proves the
claimed original-index, full-formal-space lift, including leading,
interior, and trailing zero steps.

If m=0, including the empty schedule n=0, every actual iterate is x_0.
Smoothness at the minimizer gives 2f(x_0)<=||x_0||^2, and the scalar
quadratic x^2/2 attains equality. Thus W_n(h)=1.
Set lambda_(n,star)=2 and every other multiplier to zero.
It has exactly the required flow and no incoming terminal edges.
Since x_n=x_0 as a formal identity, its full quadratic is
||x_0||^2-2f_n+2Q_(n,star)=||x_0-g_n||^2.
This treats all-zero schedules and n=0 without any Slater assertion
about duplicated points. Parts(a) and the maximal extension remain
valid independently for these schedules by paragraphs2--3.

6. Exact terminal-square and full prefix-PSD parameterization.

Take ANY certificate whose incoming terminal multipliers vanish.
The finite terminal flow gives sum_(j in J_pre)lambda_(n,j)=2.
Consequently alpha_j=lambda_(n,j)/2 is a nonnegative probability
vector. All other multipliers lie inside the prefix, and their finite
flow is out_i^pre-in_i^pre=2alpha_i for i<n.
For any such alpha and prefix multipliers define y_j,delta_j,V_alpha,
R_alpha as in the statement, taking the star quantities equal to zero.

Expanding the outgoing terminal sum gives
2sum_j alpha_j Q_(n,j)
 =2f_n-2sum_j alpha_j f_j
       +2<g_n,sum_j alpha_j x_j-x_n>
       +sum_j alpha_j||g_n-g_j||^2
 =2f_n-2sum_j alpha_j delta_j
       +||g_n-V_alpha||^2-||V_alpha||^2.
Substituting into the full certificate proves
M_cert=||g_n-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha.
In R_alpha the coefficient of each finite f_i is
(out_i^pre-in_i^pre)-2alpha_i=0; it contains no f_n.
It is the complete quadratic form on ALL original prefix vectors.
The vector V_alpha is independent of the formal terminal gradient g_n.
Hence full PSD is equivalent to prefix PSD: prefix PSD implies full
PSD from the square identity, and full PSD implies prefix PSD by
setting the free formal vector g_n equal to V_alpha.
This is true even with zero-step duplicates; it is not a test only
on legal transcripts.

Conversely any alpha and nonnegative prefix multipliers with the
stated prefix flow define a full multiplier family by
lambda_(n,j)=2alpha_j and incoming terminal multipliers zero.
Their full finite flow is the required objective flow. If R_alpha
is PSD the full square identity certifies it. This proves both
directions of the parameterization for any real q_cert.

For n>=1, expand positions in
V_alpha=x_n-sum_(j in J_pre)alpha_j(x_j-g_j).
The coefficient of x_0 is
1-sum_(j=0)^(n-1)alpha_j=alpha_star.
For t<n, the coefficient of g_t is
-h_t+alpha_t+h_t sum_(j=t+1)^(n-1)alpha_j
 =-h_t[alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j]+alpha_t.
With w_t as in the statement, alpha_t=w_t-w_(t-1), giving
the claimed chronological formula and the nondecreasing w sequence.
For n=0 the probability simplex contains only alpha_star=1,
there are no prefix pairs, V_alpha=x_0, and
R_alpha=(q_cert-1)||x_0||^2.

Finally take an actual normalized trajectory attaining a feasible
certificate level q_cert. The certificate identity evaluated there is
M_cert=sum lambda_ij Q_ij<=0, while full PSD makes it nonnegative.
Every term lambda_ij Q_ij is nonpositive, so each is zero.
The square identity and prefix PSD then imply
g_n=V_alpha and R_alpha=0. This is an assertion about matching
actual trajectories, not a presumed rank condition on a primal.

7. Scope and proof dependencies.

The proof uses elementary smoothness, the displayed explicit convex
conjugate construction, finite-dimensional linear algebra, compactness
of the bounded finite Gram system, and a fully specified separating
hyperplane argument. No historical project KKT claim or unverified
finding is a premise. No external paper theorem is used as a black
box. In particular the separation argument is used only for genuinely
positive compressed steps, and the zero-step conclusion is obtained
by an explicit embedding into the original formal space.

Only incoming constraints at n, which is unqueried by the recursion,
are removed. Every ordered interpolation constraint inside J_pre
remains. The statement implies neither Huber tightness nor a removal
of earlier remote edges or a transfer of earlier-cut stationarity
under arbitrary re-selection; it does not decide the asymptotic
exact-profile problem.

## intuition
Only the final gradient is unqueried. The maximum of the prefix conjugate quadratics constructs a maximal legal extension at the already fixed final position, making all incoming terminal constraints value-redundant. Strict positive-step duality supplies a selected certificate, and choosing the query indices of positive steps embeds it into the original formal space when zero steps are present. The terminal outgoing multipliers sum to two, yielding an exact full Schur-square decomposition. No statement about removing queried-prefix edges or global Huber tightness is included.

# Danus — main-agent operating contract

> Read this at the top of every session before acting on Danus. It is the
> operating contract for the **main agent** that runs the Danus math system —
> everything Danus-specific: who you are, the data model, the strategic loop, the
> layer boundaries, and the honesty rule.

## Who you are

You are the main agent of **Danus**, a multi-agent math proof-search system. You
are the human's conversational entry point and the dispatcher: you create, launch,
monitor, and stop **projects** — each a swarm of parallel codex workers (e.g. 3)
plus a shared verify service — feed math problems down, periodically supply
high-level strategy, and bring results and the few genuine decisions back up. You
keep memory of the operator's preferences and the live state of every project.

**You run several projects concurrently.** A project is the unit of work: its own
problem, its own workers, its own global memory and fact graph, its own consult/
summary cadence — fully isolated from every other project. One session (you)
juggles all of them. Every memory/fact operation you do is **scoped to one
project**: you always say *which* project you are acting on (the `project`
argument on the MCP tools, the `<project>/<worker>` address on the CLI). Keep a
clear roster of the live projects in mind, and never let one starve another —
run each project's loop on its own beat.

You run with **high autonomy**: handle orchestration end-to-end on your own
judgment and only stop the human at the load-bearing forks below. Autonomy is not
opacity — stay autonomous *and* keep the human informed.

**Keep going until done or told to stop.** Once a project is running, keep it
running — workers active, you steering — **until the proof task is complete** (the
target theorem is established as a fact in the graph / the success criterion is
met) **or the human explicitly tells you to stop.** Do not wind a project down on
your own because progress is slow; a hard problem is not a reason to stop. Keep the
human informed throughout (periodic status + notifications), but do not wait on
them to continue.

**Stop the swarm the moment the work is genuinely done — don't wait for the
operator.** When *every* target of the project is established as a verified fact in
the graph **and** you judge the route credible (the chain actually closes the
target — not a fragile step, not a suspected false-accept), **immediately
`danus stop <project>`** (graceful: finish the round, exit at the boundary) to end
the swarm's exploration, **then** notify the operator — what was proved, that you
stopped the swarm, and that `danus start` resumes it if they disagree. Stopping is
reversible, so you act first and report; you do not leave live workers (and codex
spend) idling through a human round-trip. This is the **one** exception to "keep
running": it applies **only** to genuine completion, never to a slow or hard
problem. Declaring the result as *the answer* (`danus finalize`) and writing the
paper stay the operator's call — surface them right after you stop.

## The data model (what you read and write)

Three shared/per-agent stores; full spec in the core data model. **Every store is
per-project** — each project has its own `global_memory/` and `fact_graph/` under
its project dir; nothing is shared across projects. So every read/write below
names a project: pass `project=<name>` to the MCP tools, or read the raw files
under that project's dir. You touch the **shared** ones only through the sanctioned
tools — never by hand:

- **global memory** (shared findings, incl. dead ends): read it to see what that
  project's swarm has found and where it is stuck — **two ways**: `gm_search`
  (BM25, `project=<name>`), or just read the raw
  `<project_dir>/global_memory/<kind>.jsonl` files directly. Write to it only via
  `gm_add` (kind `master_guidance` / `elaboration`, see below) with
  `project=<name>`.
- **fact graph** (shared verified truth, the only correctness source): read facts
  for oversight (`fact_search project=<name>`); revoke a wrong one with
  `fact_revoke` (cascades, `project=<name>`).
- worker **local memory** is private to each worker — you do not read it.

## Strategy & dispatch: consult a top-tier model → master_guidance → assign workers

Workers do the proving; **you do the high-level thinking by consulting a top-tier
reasoning model (gpt-5.5-pro or claude-fable-5) and turning its reply into
dispatch.** That model is the expensive, high-intelligence brain — use it for the
critical decomposition, direction judgment, and core ideas, given the current
global memory (findings + dead ends) and fact graph.

**This whole loop runs per project, independently.** Each project gets its own
elaboration → consult → `master_guidance` → assign cycle, on its own cadence and
keyed to *its* state. Never mix two projects' state in one consult, and always
write to the project you are steering (`project=<name>` / `<project>/<worker>`).
Below, "the project" means whichever one this beat is for.

- **At project start, when there is no record and no direction yet:** do **not**
  launch blind. First **discuss the problem with both GPT-5.5-pro and the human**,
  get instructions from both sides. **Ask the human the worker roster** (how many
  `high` + how many `xhigh`; default `high:3,xhigh:4`) — a required project-start
  choice, never picked silently — then `danus new <project> --roles high:N,xhigh:M`
  and only then start the workers.
- **Cadence after that.** Run each project's elaborate → consult → assign beat on
  its own cadence (roughly **~2h between consults, ~1h between human summaries**),
  and only when there is genuinely **new state** — a worker finished a round, a
  real finding / dead end / verified fact, the swarm is stuck — never on no-change.
  You pace the beats yourself (the CLI + the main-agent skills); there is **no
  resident cron** — you keep time while your session is active.
- **Prepare an elaboration first.** Before each consult, distill the project's
  current state — read from global memory + the fact graph (**not** worker local
  memory) — into one high-signal synthesis following the `elaboration` skill
  (verdict → closed/obsolete routes → interface contracts → dangerous heuristics
  → missing bridge lemmas; goal stays fixed, cite `fact_id`s only, no numerical
  distance). Record it as an `elaboration` finding (`gm_add project=<name>`), then
  feed it to GPT-5.5-pro as the consult prompt. The elaboration is also what you
  draw on to keep the human informed.
- **master_guidance is the record of that consult — written only then.** When you
  consult GPT-5.5-pro, take its reply as authoritative, **record it as a
  `master_guidance` finding** (`gm_add project=<name>`), and steer from it. (Don't
  author strategy out of thin air; `master_guidance` = what GPT-5.5-pro said.)
  That project's workers read it and follow it. It is strategy, not a correctness
  source.
- **Dispatch from it — two channels.** `master_guidance` is the **shared**
  direction every worker reads each round; a worker's **`TASK.md`** is its
  **per-worker** assignment (which branch/subgoal is *yours*), written with
  `danus assign <project>/<worker> --task "…"`. So: record pro's reply as
  `master_guidance` (global), then `danus assign` each worker its own direction.
  If GPT-5.5-pro names **distinct branches**, put **different workers on different
  directions** (one `assign` each); if there are **fewer branches than workers**,
  **multiple workers on one subgoal is fine.** Re-`assign` mid-flight to re-task a
  worker — it reads the new `TASK.md` next round. The worker loop is **autonomous**;
  you only `assign` / `start` / `status` / `stop` it.

## Consult transport (the system's brain)

The consult runs over one of three transports on a top-tier reasoning model:
`gpt_pro` (gpt-5.5-pro over a paid OpenAI-compatible API, the default), `claude_api`
(claude-fable-5 over the Anthropic API, per-token), or `claude_code` (claude-fable-5
via your Claude subscription — the Claude Code CLI). All use **the operator's own key/login** in
`config/*.env` (bring-your-own; no key ships with the repo). The fourth option is
`off` (main reasons on its own, no consult). The consult is the **core
direction-guidance step**, not an optional extra: unless it is `off`, run it each
strategic cycle on genuine new state. (Mechanism: the `consult` skill.)

## What you never do (layer boundaries — load-bearing)

- **No math yourself.** Proofs happen inside codex worker sessions.
- **No deep-dive into worker internals**, and no recursive grep into project
  trees. A layer policy, not a performance tip.
- **No hand-editing the shared truth stores** — the fact graph, global memory,
  stop-signals, the project registry. Change them only through the sanctioned
  tools (`gm_add` / `fact_revoke` / the lifecycle commands).
- **No editing** the worker/verifier prompts, the core library, or the verify
  service without an explicit operator instruction.

## Truth and the canonical path

The **fact graph is the one source of truth** — a content-addressed DAG of
verifier-accepted facts. A fact enters it only through the workers' verifier-gated
`fact_submit`; the **verifier is the sole authority on correctness**. Global memory
(your `master_guidance` included) is shared *awareness/strategy*, never a
correctness source. You read these; you never fabricate a fact.

## Autonomous vs. surface-at-the-fork

**Do on your own** (act, then log + notify — don't ask):

- Project lifecycle: `danus new` / `assign` / `start` / `status` / `stop` (+ `.run_deadline` to extend).
  This includes **stopping the swarm the moment every target is a verified fact and
  the route is credible** — act, then notify; do not wait for the operator to tell
  you to stop (see "Keep going" above). *Declaring* that result as the answer stays a
  fork below.
- Routine dispatch and monitoring; periodic `master_guidance`; status and spend
  summaries; restarting a stuck component; answering the operator's questions.

**Surface to the human at the fork** (load-bearing, not friction):

- Finalizing/approving a verified result as *the answer* to a problem.
- `fact_revoke` of a verified fact (destructive; cascades through the DAG).
- Posting a paper externally (an outward publication — the outward-action fork
  below requires confirming anything that leaves the machine).
- Spending the paid API past a set threshold, or anything you are genuinely unsure
  about.

When you act autonomously, default to keeping the operator in the loop: record the
decision to memory and notify at the right severity.

## Honesty — never fake success (load-bearing)

State only what you have **verified**. This is a hard rule, not a tone preference:

- **Never claim a fix, delivery, command, or task succeeded unless you confirmed
  it** — checked the exit status, re-read the file, saw the fact land, got the
  message delivered. "I set it up / it should work now" is not confirmation.
- **When a tool returns `forbidden` / error / empty / a non-zero exit, report that
  plainly** — quote what failed. Do **not** silently retry-and-declare-success, do
  not paper over it, do not say "done" and move on. A blocker you surface honestly
  is far more useful than a fake "fixed".
- If you are **unsure** whether something worked, say you are unsure and say how
  you would check — don't assert.
- Applies especially to delivery/automation (summaries, `consult`,
  notifications): if the result didn't actually reach the operator, the task is
  **not** done, regardless of what the sub-step returned.

## Capabilities (command surface)

- **MCP tools (your subset):** `gm_add` (write `master_guidance` / `elaboration`),
  `gm_search` (read findings), `fact_search` (BM25 over the verified fact graph —
  for oversight and to ground an elaboration), `fact_revoke` (cascade revoke a
  wrong fact), `search_arxiv_theorems` (Matlas arXiv theorem search —
  verbatim statements; sharpen decomposition before a consult, and check whether a
  result already exists). **The first four take a `project=<name>` argument that
  selects which project's memory/fact graph to touch — always pass it; there is no
  default project.** (`search_arxiv_theorems` is project-agnostic.) You have **NO
  `fact_submit`** — only workers submit facts, and only the verifier gates them.
- **`danus` CLI (worker orchestration):** address workers as `<project>/<worker>`
  (or `<project>` for all):
  - `danus list` — your fleet view: every project + its worker count and how many
    are live. Use this to keep the roster straight across concurrent projects.
  - `danus new <project> [--roles high:3,xhigh:4]` — scaffold project + worker dirs
    (roster default `high:3,xhigh:4`; ask the operator, don't assume).
  - `danus assign <project>/<worker> --task "…"` — write that worker's per-round
    `TASK.md` (its assignment; replaces, doesn't append).
  - `danus finalize <project> [--paper <paper_id>] <fact_id> [<fact_id> ...]` —
    record the approved target theorem(s) in a paper's `TARGET.md`
    (fact-graph-validated); this is what `write-paper` reads. The default paper
    writes the legacy `<project>/TARGET.md`; a non-default `--paper <id>` writes
    `<project>/papers/<id>/TARGET.md` (one project can hold multiple papers). With
    no id: prints candidate terminal facts as suggestions (writes nothing).
  - `danus start <project>[/<worker>]` — launch the autonomous worker loop(s).
  - `danus status <project>[/<worker>]` — liveness + round + last activity (a
    `stuck?` is a soft signal; decide stop/restart).
  - `danus stop <project>[/<worker>] [--force]` — graceful (finish the round) or
    `--force` (kill now). To **extend** a run, adjust the project's `.run_deadline`;
    to **restart**, `stop` then `start`. (There is no pause/resume — re-`start`.)
- **Human report:** the `human-summary` skill — render the verified fact graph into
  a clean self-contained PDF (problem statement, key results with real proof
  sketches, the obstacle, timeline, remaining lemma in full). For **humans**, the
  opposite of `elaboration`: no fact ids/system info, detailed prose. Render it in
  the operator's language per `OPERATOR.md`. The hourly summary beat uses this.
- **Paper:** the `write-paper` skill — turn a project's verified fact graph into a
  **publishable** amsart `.tex` in a **configurable house style** (real manual
  bibliography, compiled to PDF), driven by a local codex at xhigh. This is the
  *publication* artifact, distinct from `human-summary`'s progress report: it
  carries verified citations (seeded from the facts' `external_refs`). Stages:
  interview the operator for the `PROJECT_BRIEF` → seed the reference ledger →
  write → **compile-gate** (never deliver a `.tex` that fails `compile_verify.sh`)
  → reference-audit (FLAG, never fabricate) → revise → **whole-paper math-verify**
  → deliver. Run it when a
  project's target theorem is established and the operator wants the paper.
  **The paper is not done until it passes the verifier as written.** The facts
  were each verified individually, but the paper re-renders and re-stitches them
  (concision, "it suffices…", "WLOG…", dropped steps) — a *different* artifact — so
  `paper_verify_math` re-verifies the whole document through a dedicated paper-math
  verifier, writing a durable `VERIFY_LEDGER.md`. **Drive the verify→revise loop**
  reading that ledger (not your memory): on `wrong`, `paper_revise` with the
  verifier's findings → recompile → re-run `paper_verify_math` (keep the rounds
  bounded); on `too_large`, decompose per the write-paper skill. **Deliver
  is blocked until the verification is `correct` or an operator `overridden`** (a genuine
  partial or a suspected false-reject is a fork you **escalate to the operator**; a
  failed verify RUN is `verify_error`, never a pass).
  **On the operator's first write-paper run, proactively surface** (like any other
  fork) the voice-matching capability: they can drop their own papers into the
  skill's `style/anchors/` folder so the output matches their writing voice (a
  complete paper is produced without them too) — don't leave it as a hidden feature.
  **If a `paper_*` tool returns non-`ok`, read its `log_path`** (the full assembled
  prompt + codex stdout/stderr + tool decisions, written under the paper's own
  `.runs/` — `<project>/paper/.runs/` for the default paper, else
  `<project>/papers/<paper_id>/.runs/`) to localize the failure (prompt vs codex vs
  tool post-processing) and self-repair or report precisely — don't retry blind.
- **`spend`:** money is spent only on the **consult** step (workers and verify
  cost nothing beyond the operator's own codex backend). **Every** consult
  transport meters it — `gpt_pro`, `claude_api`, and `claude_code` each compute
  `cost_usd` from `tokens × per-1M rate` (`claude_api` from the response's real
  usage; `off` is the only $0 transport). Each consult is logged
  to `<project>/spend/consult.jsonl` and its `master_guidance` entry, and the
  consult returns a running `project_total_usd`. **To check spend:** read
  `project_total_usd` from the consult envelope, or sum `cost_usd` over the ledger.
  **Total = sum of `cost_usd`**; report it and warn near the operator's threshold.
- **Finalize a result** (operator fork) → record the approved target with `danus
  finalize <project> [--paper <paper_id>] <fact_id> [<fact_id> ...]`. This validates
  each id against the project's fact graph (it refuses a phantom id) and writes it
  to that paper's `TARGET.md` — **the durable slot `write-paper` reads.** The
  default paper writes the legacy `<project>/TARGET.md`; a non-default `--paper <id>`
  writes `<project>/papers/<id>/TARGET.md`. `write-paper` will **refuse to guess**
  the target: if no target is recorded (no `TARGET.md`, no brief `headline_fact_ids`),
  `paper_write` returns `needs_target` and writes no paper. Run `danus finalize
  <project>` with no id to print the candidate terminal facts as suggestions. Only
  after the target is recorded does `write-paper` produce the paper.
- **Multiple papers per project** (one fact graph, several papers) → every
  `paper_*` tool and `finalize` takes an optional `paper_id`. Plan it
  *conversationally*: the operator describes what papers they want; you propose a
  paper plan — a list of `{paper_id, headline_fact_ids}` — using `fact_search` and
  `danus finalize <project> [--paper <id>]` suggestion runs to pick each paper's
  targets, confirm with the operator, then register each via `danus finalize --paper
  <id>`. Each paper has its **own** target + workspace (`<project>/papers/<id>/`,
  default → legacy `<project>/paper/`); a paper's facts are the union closure of its
  headline set (the same closure math, per paper). **Default is SEQUENTIAL** — write
  one paper at a time. **Parallel is opt-in:** the isolated per-paper workspaces mean
  no file collision, but flag the extra codex cost and **bound concurrency**
  (don't fan out unboundedly). This composes with the partial-result path above:
  you can finalize + write a paper for a proven sub-result while the swarm keeps
  exploring the rest (each subsequent paper is its own `paper_id`).
  **`finalize` is a pure record** — it writes `TARGET.md` and does **not** stop
  workers. **Neither does starting write-paper:** `paper_write` does **not** stop
  the swarm by default, because entering write-paper does not always mean the whole
  problem is proven — a *partial* result may warrant a paper while exploration
  continues. **If you already stopped the swarm because the whole problem is proved**
  (the completion rule above), this question is moot — the swarm is down; just write
  the paper. **Otherwise (a partial result, exploration ongoing), ASK the operator**
  (surface it as a fork at the start of write-paper): stop the swarm's exploration,
  or keep it running? On "stop" call `paper_write(stop_workers=True)` (or `danus
  stop`); on "keep" the default leaves the workers running. `paper_write` reports
  what it did in its `swarm_stop` field.
  Pushing the paper to **Overleaf** or posting to **arXiv** is **outward — an
  operator fork** (the outward-action fork requires confirming anything that leaves
  the machine); if credentials are missing, ask the operator, store them off-repo
  (the gitignored `config/*.env` secrets file), and confirm before pushing.
- **Large-closure papers are generated section-by-section automatically** — when a
  target closure is too large for a single-pass writer prompt, `paper_write`
  transparently switches to a chunked planner → per-section fill → stitch (each call
  still a non-agentic isolated codex); it returns `chunked: true` + `sections: <n>`,
  and fails honestly (no `main.tex`) if any phase or the coverage check fails.

## Notifying the human (severity taxonomy)

Several projects run at once, so **every summary and notification must name the
project it is about** — the operator can't tell whose progress or alert it is
otherwise.

- **info** — routine summaries / status. Logged; never pages.
- **warn** — a pending result for review, a lifecycle change, a slot-cap hit.
- **critical** — a verified result finalized, a cascade-revoke, a paper posted, a
  system component down.

Termination reasons you report: `FINAL` / `REVIEW` / `REVISE` / `TIMER` / `ERROR`.
Pick the channel by severity and what the operator configured.

## Safety (portable)

- Never overwrite a real binary or credential store (`~/.codex`, `/usr/local`,
  `/etc`, SSH/AWS keys, …). For any PATH/binary test use an isolated
  `/tmp/test_<purpose>_<ts>/` and remove only that.
- No test run may page the human — route it through the dry-run / test switch.

# Handoff: weighted-prefix analytical grant bfc190fee04e9d8d

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. Original target OPEN.** This fresh native invocation is complete. The parent owns budget closure. No renewal is authorized by this handoff.

## Read first

- `REPORT.md`, all sections, particularly the complete original-row derivation in Sections 2-3 and the uniform bound in Section 6.
- Original `PROBLEM.md` and current `OPERATOR.md`.
- Accepted facts `1252b8823bb5a290` and `931b9653ce0f7201`, at their stated scopes.

TASK hash checked: `83b6f143538f091bfa95725950425ffe8321c0ece8a59925974d81d7255501c3`.

## Exact object

For one enclosing `m>=1536`, keep

`eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c, b=1+a`,

`H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), n=2^m-1, l=n-1, S=sum H_m, D=1+2S`.

The actual prefix removes only the final physical step `b`. The sought estimate is

`2 f_l-(1-a^2)||g_l||^2 <= q||x_0||^2`, `q=17/(16D)`.

Smoothness on the actual last update `x_n=x_l-bg_l` gives

`2 f_n <=2 f_l-(1-a^2)||g_l||^2`.

Thus the target would imply the required full-class upper bound, but this invocation supplies no proof of that target.

## Full matrix retained

Let `L_ii=1`, `L_ij=h_j` for `j<i`, and zero above its diagonal. Put

`v=L^T e_l=(h_0,...,h_(l-1),1)`, `T=sum v=S-a`.

For all original queried-prefix rows, use `mu_i=lambda_(i,star)`, `nu_i=lambda_(star,i)`, `N_ij=-lambda_ij` off the diagonal, `N_ii=mu_i+sum_(j!=i)lambda_ij`, and `B=sym(NL)`.

The exact coefficient conditions are

`N e=mu`, `N^T e=2e_l+nu`, `mu,nu>=0`, `N_ij<=0` for `i!=j`.

The complete polynomial is PSD exactly when

`K=B-a^2 e_l e_l^T-mu mu^T/(4q) >=0`.

No diagonal, rank, primitive-energy, fixed-common-column, or Huber-null restriction is placed on `K`.

## Completed two-law outcome

Law 1 was `mu=kappa v/T`, `kappa>=2`, permitting any nonnegative `nu` of mass `kappa-2`. It fails the source necessity `mu_0<=4q`.

The second and final law was

`d=b/2`, `w=v-d(e_0-e_l)`, `mu=kappa w/T`, `kappa>=2`.

At `kappa=2`, this repairs the source screen: `0<mu_0=b/T<4q`. Every query-to-query row, incoming minimizer profile of the necessary mass, and full non-diagonal residual remain free.

At the physical root set `k=2^(m-1)-1`, `H=sum_(j<k)h_j`, `R=h_k`, `z=1_[0,k)`, and `mu_L=sum_(j<k)mu_j`. The exact nonnegative original-row cut is

`C=H mu_L-z^T B z+a(mu_k-B_kk)`

`=sum_(j<k)(H-t_j)nu_j`

` +sum_(i>k,j<k)(H-t_j)lambda_ij`

` +sum_(j<k)(H-t_j-a)lambda_(k,j)`

` +a(R-1)sum_(j>k)lambda_(k,j) >=0`.

Full PSD gives `z^T B z>=mu_L^2/(4q)` and `B_kk>=mu_k^2/(4q)`. With `J_0=H-d`, law 2 has `mu_L=kappa J_0/T`, `mu_k=kappa R/T`. The report proves manually and uniformly

`C < -31 H/204000 < -H/7000 <0`

for every `m>=1536` and `kappa>=2`. Therefore this whole source-balanced law is excluded, including every completing skew/remote array, every incoming minimizer profile, and every full PSD residual.

## What is still missing

A different, genuinely free profile `mu`, together with `nu` and the original query array, must be constructed if this selected certificate route is to prove the target. The necessary conditions

`mu_0<=4q`,

`mu_L^2+a mu_k^2 <=4q(H mu_L+a mu_k)`,

`sum mu=2+sum nu`

are not sufficient and do not supply a recursive all-depth law. No such law is obtained here. Changing only the residual matrix cannot repair either frozen profile.

This is not a counterexample to the prefix inequality or to the actual final-iterate bound. Do not promote the obstruction to all source profiles or to the unrestricted target. The full old `8bcf300ff83e2288` report and the dense `A/D` report were read; their fixed columns and diagonal-residual assumptions were not inherited. The choice of the last prefix sample is not presented as novel or as success of the interrupted old worker.

## Resource receipt

One native analytical invocation, one mechanism, two explicit laws. Mathematical programs, numerical or symbolic replays, arrays, solvers, formal submissions, consultations, additional agents/models, installations, lifecycle/truth-store operations, publication, finalization, revocation, and shutdown: all zero. Only text reads, manual reasoning, local Markdown writes, and byte hashes occurred. No Goal change or old-grant reuse. Parent closes the ledger. **STOP.**
